  the job lists the absent layers in its result (`absent`) and says them in one line for the pass; a country with no export at all is
  `skipped` / `absent` and its zone `done` (it was `failed`, `http`, 404, which made the whole fetch « partial » for nothing to do).
- **Remembered per AIRAC cycle**, outside `data/` (`tools/.openaip_absent.json`, ignored by git): the next pass of the same cycle does not
  ask again; a new cycle asks everything once more (openAIP may have added a layer).
- **Still failures**, said per file and in the console: a 5xx, a time-out, an unreadable answer; a 403 too in the console (S3 answers 403
  for a key it hides; the job treats it as absent, as before).
- **Data module**: the source state `skipped` with the new code `absent` (its generic « ignorée » until Réglages gives it its words).
- **Bench**: K60 (absent layers, one line, the zone done, the cache per cycle and outside data/, no second request, a new cycle asks again,
  a 503 a failure, the console of `outbound` quiet for an expected 404 only); K54 updated (es and us without export: absent, not failed).

## 1 November 2026 — a 304 Not Modified is « unchanged », never a failure

- **The user's log after 822a0ffbb**: « [sortant] échec : s3.openaip.net/…/de_asp.geojson : HTTP 304 » (and `be_asp`, `es_asp`). urllib raises
  a 304 as an `HTTPError`; `aero_fetch.get` already read it as « the copy is good », but the launcher's opener wrote every `HTTPError` first.
- **Now**: `aero_fetch` opens through `outbound.opener(quiet=…)` (the launcher's own API, nothing changed in `outbound.py`) with 304 always
  quiet, and 404 / 410 too for an optional export (`absent_ok`). Only `aero_fetch` sends conditional requests in the tools.
- **A 304 without a copy**: `get(url, since, copy=path)` returns « unchanged » only when the copy is there; otherwise it asks again without
  condition (`Cache-Control: no-cache`) rather than fail on a file it does not have; a 304 to that request is a real error, said.
- **Bench**: K61 (a 304 keeps the copy, bytes and date, one conditional request, nothing in the console; a 304 with no copy asks again
  without condition and writes the file; a second 304 is an error said once); K54, K57 and K60 follow the new opener.

## 1 November 2026 — the build says for which cycle it built

- **The user rebuilt the base several times** with the SIA files of 2026-10-01 (2610) while 2611 was in force: the build wrote 2610 again, the
  base stayed out of date, and nothing said why.
- **Now** the result of `base/build` carries `cycles` (per built zone) beside the existing `cycle`, `current` (the cycle in force) and `stale`;
  the journal says « sources du cycle …, cycle en vigueur … ». **No refusal**: rebuilding a past cycle stays possible. The Données module
  (Réglages) words it; it reads the fields tolerantly.
- **One cycle for the whole build**: the only dated sources are France's SIA files, which every zone reads (the FIR outlines); the others
  (openAIP, ENAIRE, NATS…) carry no AIRAC date. `cycles` is per zone so that a later per-zone rule fits the same shape.
- **Bench**: K46 (cycle, cycles of the built zones, current, stale and its journal line; the AIRAC calendar and `cycle_of`).

## 1 November 2026 — every read answers or fails: aircraft() and the registry bounded too

- **A real pilot, enrolled today, was stuck on Fuel › Route** (the whole screen inert): the GitLab API did not answer, `aircraft()` never
  resolved, and Fuel waited on it. Fuel no longer waits (Features SOG, f3d4e82a9: 4 s for the flight, 8 s for the registry), but the cause was
  here: the bound of 31 October (`readWait`) covered a zone's parts only.
- **Now every read with `wait: true` and every listing of aerodb has the bound**, except the three reads A15 keeps unbounded (the list of
  zones, the manifest, a zone's meta), the read before a write and the offline download. At the bound: « nothing » (an empty registry over
  the types base, no procedures, no cities…), the shared read abandoned (`reader.abandon`), and **no memo built during a fallen bound is
  kept** (`lateN`, a counter of fallen bounds: the registry, a procedures entry, the index, a cycle choice, the cities): the next question
  asks again, never an empty answer remembered as true.
- **The registry** (`registry.js`): `readAll(reader, { wait, late })` and `create({ readWait, onLate })` bound each read and listing the same
  way; without these options (V2 tools, benches) nothing changes.
- **Bench**: K62 (the fleet read and the `airline/` listing held: `aircraft()` answers at the bound, 600 ms here, with an empty registry and
  the types base; freed: the next `aircraft()` reads again, gets the fleet, and is then kept; the held read abandoned) — red before the
  correction (still pending after 8 s).

## 1 November 2026 — a zone's cycle is its own dated source's, not the SIA's for all

- **Features SOG on the user's workstation**: `cycle_of()` dated the whole build — every zone, cities, relief, openAIP, UK, ENAIRE — by the newest
  French SIA file; with `data/sia` still in 2610, every « Construire » rewrote 2610 everywhere, and the status said « out of date » for sets that
  have no AIRAC at all. 41e34a752 (one `cycle` for all, `stale` global) only said it.
- **Now** (`tools/aero_cycles.py`, one module for `base/fetch` and `base/build`): the dated sources are those whose file names carry their cycle
  — the SIA (France), NATS (`EG_…_AAAAMMJJ`, United Kingdom), ENAIRE (`LE_Amdt_A_AAAA_NN`, Spain: AIRAC amendment NN read as cycle AANN; an
  amendment number without « A_ », `LE_Amdt_2026_411`, says no cycle). A zone's cycle is its own dated source's; a zone without one has no
  cycle (never out of date) and is written under the cycle in force. The page is told each zone's cycle (`cycles` in the claim, checked like
  the rest; `SiaBase.buildZones({ cycles })`: absent, the output is the page's, byte for byte, as before).
- **Result**: `cycles` per zone (null without AIRAC), `folders` (where written), `blocking` (the zone, its source, its past cycle), `stale`,
  `sources` (the cycle held per dated source; also in `base/fetch`'s plan and result), `builtOn`. The Données module words them (Réglages).
- **Assumption to watch**: ENAIRE's « A_AAAA_NN » is read as cycle AANN (A 10/2026 ↔ 2610 on the user's files of October); if ENAIRE skips a
  cycle one year, the numbering would drift and this reading must change.
- **Bench**: K63 (fr from its SIA, gb from its NATS file of 2611, de without AIRAC under the cycle in force; only fr blocks; the manifest per
  zone), K64 (calendar, names, the source table agrees, the report, base/fetch's plan and end), K44 / K46 / K49 follow the per-zone folders.

## 1 November 2026 — the build's count of sources is the build's, not each zone's

- **The user saw « construction (90 / 90 sources) » on all 26 zone lines** (Réglages): the builder reads every source once for all the zones
  (one browser, one pass) and says its progress for the whole build (zone « * »); `base/build` copied it on every zone being built, and each
  zone started at `0 / 0`.
- **Now** the global count goes once in the job's data, `inputs: { done, total }` (named so, not `sources`, which `base/fetch`'s view uses for
  the cycles held per dated source); a zone gets `done` / `total` only from a count of its own (`{ zone, sources }`, `{ zone, n }`), and has no
  such keys otherwise. The builder gives none per zone today; the Données module shows a zone's figure only when `total > 0`.
- **Bench**: K65 (two zones with different counts each keep their own, a zone without one has none, the global count once in `inputs` and
  never on a zone); K46 (no count on the zones, `inputs` 1 / 2).

## 1 November 2026 — a class E space stops under the class A floor (parser version 21)

- **The user, on the cross-section of KSET → KDOV**: « est-on sûrs du plafond des zones US ? ». In `sia/base/us/2026-10-01/airspaces.json`,
  3 158 class E spaces (« … CLASS E5 », floor « 1200 ft ASFC ») carried `upper: 'FL 600'`, openAIP's value for « unlimited »; in the United
  States class E stops below 18 000 ft MSL, class A runs from FL180 to FL600.
- **Rule**: a class E space never goes above its State's class A floor. A table by State (`CLASS_A_FLOOR_FT`, openAIP's country code; the
  FAA's Class Airspace is the US): US and CA at 18 000 ft. A class E whose ceiling is at or above the floor, or unlimited, gets `upper:
  '17999 ft AMSL'` and **`capped: 'classA'`** (a field, not a free text, for the Map to word); a ceiling below the floor, the other classes and
  the States not in the table keep the source's ceiling. The table lives with the reader that applies it (`shared/aero_sources.js`).
- **Canada**: the southern domestic airspace's class A starts at FL180 (higher further north); 18 000 ft is the conservative value.
- **Version 21** (`shared/sia_base.js` VERSION, `AeroCatalogue.PARSER_VERSION`): the zones built before show « outdated » until rebuilt.
- **Bench**: K66 (an E5 at FL 600 → 17 999 ft capped; an E at 2 500 ft unchanged; D and B unchanged; Canada capped, Germany not; at FL180
  capped, at FL175 kept; the FAA's class E too).

## 2 November 2026 — what aerodb reads from data/ is written by any pilot (security audit 157, § 10.36)

- **Rule** (written by the security session, to keep for every reader): since § 10.36 every file of `data/` that aerodb reads —
  `aircraft/sheets/`, `airline/fleet.json`, the reference, the published bases under `sia/base/`, `proc/` — can be written by any pilot of the
  group. So: numbers are bounded (`PROFILE_FIELDS` and its bounds for the sheets), keys are read through own-property lookups (`own()`,
  `Object.hasOwn`, never `obj[key]` on a prototype), strings are never taken as a path (`fileOf` sanitises a sheet id; a fleet `sheetId` is only
  a key in the loaded sheets), and an unreadable file is said and ignored (`null`, never an exception that stops the reader).
- **How it is applied**: nothing to change today; each new reader of a `data/` file is checked against these four points in its review, and the
  check is written in its entry here.

## 5 November 2026 — lot C: data, cycles and sources (SOG-0026, 0022, 0027, 0025, 0021)

### SOG-0026 — « toujours dépassé » after rebuilding France: the real cause

- **Reproduced on the user's workstation** (the real `sia/base` of `data/`, read only): France was rebuilt for 2611 (`fr/2026-10-29`), every other zone
  was still published under `2026-10-01`. The module computes the state of a source of the base (`rowOf`, `modules/data/model_screen.js`) from the
  **oldest published folder cycle among the zones the source feeds** and calls `stale-cycle` anything older than the cycle in force. The SIA row said
  « à jour »; GeoNames, relief, openAIP, UK Mil, NATS, ENAIRE, FAA said « dépassé 2610 » because `geonames`, `mora` and `openaip` feed all zones
  (`'*'`, 12 published here, 11 of them at 2610) and `ukmil` / `nats` / `enaire` / `adds` feed one zone still at 2610. Answer of 1 Nov (« manual sources
  2610 ») was a different thing: those are the files, true for NATS (the user holds `EG_…20261001`), not why GeoNames or relief are « dépassés ».
- **Two faults, both about what a folder name means.** (1) A source without an AIRAC date (GeoNames, relief, openAIP, UK Mil, FAA, DIRCAM) has no
  cycle; the folder under which its zone is written is the date the zone was built under, not a cycle that can go out of date. (2) The oldest cycle
  of *all* fed zones is the wrong aggregate: one zone not rebuilt yet makes every source that feeds it « dépassé ».
- **What aerodb gives now** (`publication()`): per zone `cycleSource`: `'sia'` (fr), `'nats'` (gb), `'enaire'` (es) or `null` (no cycle). K76 (K67 renumbered twice at integration: K67 taken by the inventory case of lot E2, K70 by lot C2).
  **The rule for the module** (Réglages, `modules/data/`, not touched here): for a row of the base, the stale test counts only the fed zones whose
  `cycleSource` is not null, against the cycle of that source; when no fed zone has one, the row is never `stale-cycle`: it says « datée du
  <`at`> » (the oldest `at` of the fed zones). A zone built before parser version 21 stays « à reconstruire » (`parser`), a different state.
- **ENAIRE's cycle was read wrong in 294ffc1b8** (my own error, found while reading the page for SOG-0025): « A_2026_10 » is ENAIRE's 10th AIRAC
  amendment of 2026, not cycle 2610; the EnRoute set « A_2026_10 » is valid from 2026-10-29 (cycle 2611). The user's Spanish data **is** current:
  it was shown « 2610, dépassé » by my reading of the name. `aero_cycles.enaire_cycle` now reads the latest `validTime` start of the file (up to
  today); the name says nothing. K64 (files with and without a date, a later date ignored, an unreadable file, another set).

### SOG-0022 / SOG-0021 — LFPO ILS 07 and BOBSA 9E

- **ILS 07 had no IF and no FAF in the record because the table codes none, but the source does give both.** The ILS 07 coding page (AMDT 09/23, the
  oldest of Orly's) goes straight from the header to « See chart »; the other ILS pages (06, 24, 25, 02) code an IF. The DATA page prints, for
  runway 07, `IF ILS/LOC RWY 07  48°40'05.1'' N 002°04'58.4'' E` (the others print `REF IPOnn`) and `FAF LOC RWY 07  48°40'54.0'' N 002°09'19.8'' E`:
  positions, no names. The FAF was only inserted when an initial fix was positioned; with none in the table, nothing was. `parse_if` reads the
  unnamed initial fix; `with_threshold` inserts `IF07` (leg `IF`, `synthetic: 'if'`) when the table has none, only if it is on the final (bearing
  to the threshold within 10° of the runway), then the FAF as before: IF07 → FAF07 → RW07 → missed. K69 on the real rows of LFPO. FAF07 is the
  same place as `FPO07` of the RNP 07 page (48.681667, 2.1555): two sources, one point.
- **The names C107 and F107 are not in the source data.** They are on the IAC chart; the SIA coding pages and the AIXM 4.5 file have no point of that
  name (searched: none). The points carry `IF07` / `FAF07` and a warning says they are read from the DATA page; no name is invented.
- **A fix without a path terminator is still dropped** (`- PO410 …`, `- PO411 …`, `- PO403 …`: the first row after « See chart » of the ILS pages, whose
  leg type, course and distance the « See chart » mark hides): said in a warning, the next leg keeps `gap`. Not changed here: keeping it needs a
  point without a leg in the schema.
- **SOG-0021 (BOBSA 9E → runway 07)**: the source (SIA, `STAR_RWY07_RNAV_CODE_02`) and the data both have it: `LFPO.STAR.BOBSA9E.RW07`, BOBSA → BENAR
  → CAD → SOTIP → ODILO, status `check`, as for 02 and 06 (and `BOBSA 9W` for 20, 24, 25). What the 07 page loses is one row of CAD 9E (kept as a
  doubt). So the association exists; if Fuel does not offer it for 07, the selection by runway is Fuel's.

### SOG-0027 — openAIP: the answer, and what « Prendre le fichier téléchargé » does with it

- **The answer.** openAIP is the **fallback behind the official sources**: the build reads the SIA (France), NATS (UK), ENAIRE (Spain), the FAA… first
  and openAIP for the rest of the world (and its VFR points and runways where nothing official is read). Until now the suite fetched its own
  openAIP files (`aero_fetch`, GeoJSON); the ~100 files in the user's Downloads were **not used**: they are XML (openAIP 1.1) for the most part, a
  format the suite does not read, and 31 JSON.
- **Now** `base/pickup` takes them (INTEGRATION): GeoJSON as it is, JSON converted, XML said and not read (`skipped`), the `hot` layer set aside;
  checks on the content, never on the name alone. K68. The user's real `ci_rpp.json` converts to six VFR points that `AeroSources.geojson` reads.
  An XML 1.1 reader was not written: the same data is available as GeoJSON or JSON from the same page.

### SOG-0025 — Spain: ENAIRE's pages read (nothing imported)

- **`https://aip.enaire.es/AIP/DatosDigitales-en.html`** (read 5 Nov 2026; header « 29-OCT-26 (Incorporados AIRAC 10/26 and AMDT 412/26) »): AIXM 5.1 `.xml`
  datasets under `contenido_Datasets/`, each with a `…_Metadata.xml`, no zip, no size shown. Full sets: **Airspace** (`LE_Amdt_2026_411_AIP_DS_FULL_Airspace.xml`),
  **En-route** (`LE_Amdt_A_2026_10_AIP_DS_FULL_EnRoute.xml`), obstacles of Area 1 (`…_Obstacles_ENR.xml`), restricted and temporary aerodromes / heliports
  (`…_ADHP_Restricted.xml`, `…_ADHP_Temp.xml`), terrain (GeoTIFF, a separate page); per aerodrome: public aerodromes / heliports (`…_FULL_<ICAO>.xml`,
  one file each) and obstacles of Areas 2 to 4 (`…_Obstacles_<ICAO>.xml`, about 50). File names: `LE_Amdt_<tag>_AIP_DS_FULL_<Name>.xml`, the tag
  `A_YYYY_NN` (ENAIRE's AIRAC amendment number, not the ICAO cycle) or `YYYY_NNN` (an amendment); each set has its own tag.
- **Licence**: none stated on either page (only « Copyright © 2021 ENAIRE » on the AIP page); no registration, no condition to accept. The limitations page
  (`LE_AIP_DS_Limitaciones.html`) is technical: AIXM 5 validated since AIRAC 03/20, some attribute values longer than AIXM 5 allows, **the PDF / HTML AIP
  prevails over the AIXM**, airspace geometry in AIXM is informative. ENAIRE stays a **personal-licence** source in the catalogue (published only when the
  owner declares it), as today.
- **What the suite reads and fetches today** (`aero_fetch.enaire`, kept): the Airspace and EnRoute sets — nothing else. The user has both (Airspace
  2026-10-01 data, EnRoute valid from 2026-10-29). **The launcher can fetch them alone** (`base/fetch`, source `enaire`); « which files to take » is
  answered by the code, the user has nothing to choose.
- **Proposal, in the format of `tools/aero_cycles.py`**: `CYCLED['enaire'] = 'es'`, files `aixm5/LE_*_AIP_DS_FULL_(Airspace|EnRoute).xml`, **cycle read in
  the file** (latest `validTime` start up to today), the source's cycle the newest of the two sets, `stale` when older than the cycle in force. Done
  (see above). **Not proposed for now**: the aerodrome sets (the base already has aerodromes from openAIP; a per-aerodrome fetch is 50+ files), obstacles
  (an Area 1 set exists; the suite has its own obstacle source), terrain. **Pilot charts** (`https://aip.enaire.es/AIP/AIP-en.html`): PDFs at
  `contenido_AIP/AD/AD2/<ICAO>/LE_AD_2_<ICAO>_<TYPE>_<N>_en.pdf` (types ADC, PDC, AOC, SID, DEP, STAR, IAC, VAC, PATC, GMC…, `N` a sequence number, not a
  runway) and the aerodrome page `LE_AD_2_<ICAO>_en.html`: the links of the Route screen to the Spanish charts would parse that aerodrome page for the
  list rather than build names — a lot of its own, on the user's go.

### Verifications asked for the route `.EIDW BEPA3F BEPAN … SITET A34 LGL A34 BOBSA BOBSA9E LFPO` (lot A)

- **Zone EI**: published as `ie` (Ireland, prefix EI, cycle 2026-10-01, parser 20): 44 aerodromes including EIDW, 19 navaids, **no point** (`points.json` is
  empty: no official source of en-route points for Ireland, openAIP's `rpp` carries VFR points only). BEPAN is not in it; it is in the SID points of
  `proc/manual/EIDW.json` (BEPA3F runway 10R: …, ERVAD, NEPOD, **BEPAN** 52.526722), as for the ten other BEPA SIDs.
- **A34 LGL–BOBSA**: in the FR base of the cycle in force (`fr/2026-10-29/airways.json`): the segment `BOBSA → LGL` (18.3 NM, 6500–19500 ft), next to
  `LGL → LISEU` and `BENAR → BOBSA`. The segments are stored in one direction (a: BOBSA, b: LGL); a route read **from LGL to BOBSA** goes against
  the stored order, which the resolver of the route must accept.

### Ireland: the official source, read (nothing imported; asked by Features SOG's reading of the user's base)

- **The zone `ie` today** is an openAIP export alone (cycle 2026-10-01): 44 aerodromes, 19 navaids, **0 points, 0 airways**; the SID BEPA4E of EIDW is read
  (its points carry coordinates) but BEPAN is in no base. There is no official source of points and airways for Ireland in the suite.
- **The official source** is AirNav Ireland's eAIP (on behalf of the Irish Aviation Authority's successor, the CAA of Ireland), read on 5 Nov 2026: current issue
  effective 29 Oct 2026 at `https://www.airnav.ie/AIRAC/2026-10-29-AIRAC/html/index.html` (the AIM page, `…/air-traffic-management/aeronautical-information-management`,
  lists the issues; the folder names of past issues are not uniform, so the address of an issue is read from that page, not built); next issue 26 Nov 2026. An
  XHTML frameset, sections as `eAIP/EI-<SECTION>-en-IE.html` (`EI-ENR-4.4-…`, `EI-ENR-3.3-…`, `EI-AD-2.EIDW-…`), charts as PDFs.
- **What it gives, parseable HTML tables with DMS coordinates**: **ENR 4.4** (name-code designators: name, coordinates `DDMMSS(.s)N DDDMMSS(.s)W`, route, remarks),
  **ENR 3.3** (RNAV routes L18, L149, L975, M17, M145, N34, P600, P620, Q36, Q37: points, coordinates, tracks, distances, limits, « Westbound Only »…),
  **AD 2** (runway thresholds in AD 2.12, navaids in AD 2.19). **Not in it**: ARINC 424 coding tables (no path terminator, no per-leg coordinates) — AD 2.22 is
  narrative, the SID / STAR / approaches are charts (PDF): the reading of procedures (what `proc_sia.py` does for France) has no equivalent. **No machine-readable dataset**
  (AIXM, ARINC 424, CSV) was found; GEN 3.1 and `airnav.ie/publications`, the likely places for a data product list, were not read.
- **Licence**: no reuse wording in what was read (GEN 0.1 only says who publishes; the site's disclaimer is about accuracy). Unverified, to read
  (the eAIP cover page, GEN 1.x, `airnav.ie/publications`) before any import: « no licence found » is not permission.
- **What it would take for `ie` to have its points like France**: a reader of ENR 4.4 and ENR 3.3 (HTML tables, a layout of its own, in the manner of `proc_layouts.py`)
  writing `points` and `airways` for the zone `ie` with the eAIP issue as its dated source (`CYCLED` + a date from the folder / page title); the SIDs and STARs of EIDW
  stay read from the charts (`proc_ead` / OCR), as today, and BEPAN comes from ENR 4.4. Cost: one reader and its fixtures, a source entry (personal
  licence until the terms are read), the zone's cycle. Not started: the user's go, and the licence first.

## 5 November 2026 — lot C2: Ireland, Spain's charts, the raw SIA files, the Little Navmap follow

Each decision below is reversible and was taken without asking (the user is away); what to undo is written with it.

### Ireland (zone `ie`): the source, its licence, its fixtures

- **Source `airnav`**, kind download, **personal licence** (published only if the owner declares it, like ENAIRE and NATS): no reuse licence was found. What was read (5 Nov 2026):
  the eAIP's GEN 0.1 says « The AIP Ireland is published by the AirNav Ireland on behalf of the Civil Aviation Authority of Ireland for EISN FIR », nothing about reuse; the site's
  disclaimer (`https://www.airnav.ie/disclaimer`) says AirNav Ireland « give no guarantees, undertakings or warranties concerning the accuracy, completeness or up to date nature of the
  information provided » — about accuracy, not a licence. The eAIP cover page returned no content (a redirect), GEN 1.x and `airnav.ie/publications` were not read. « No licence found » is not
  permission: the compiled data (`sia/base/ie/…`) stay in the private data repository, never in the code repository; to undo, drop `airnav` from `EXTRA` (`shared/aero_zones.js`,
  `tools/regen/base_sources.py`) and the zone goes back to openAIP alone.
- **What it gives and what it does not**: ENR 4.4 points (167) and ENR 3.3 routes (L18, L149, L975, M17, M145, N34, P600, P620, Q36, Q37: 31 segments, with limits and direction remarks left
  out) — measured on the 29 Oct 2026 issue. **No procedures** (SIDs, STARs, approaches are charts): BEPAN and the SID points of EIDW stay read from the charts; with ENR 4.4 BEPAN is also a point of the zone.
  The `ie` base had 0 points and 0 airways.
- **Format**: a GeoJSON of two kinds of Feature (`sog: 'point'`, `sog: 'airway'`), read by `AeroSources.geojson` (K71), written by `tools/airnav_eaip.py` under the date of the edition
  (`airnav_ie_enr_AAAAMMJJ.geojson`: its cycle, `tools/aero_cycles.py`; `aerodb.CYCLED_ZONES.ie = 'airnav'`). The address of the edition is read on the AIM page, never built (the folders of past
  issues do not follow one pattern) and checked before any request (K70). The parser is a regular-expression reading of the page's markup (`SP-<ID>-LAT/-LONG`, `Significant-point-description`,
  `Route-segment-*`): a page that gives no point or no segment is refused and said (« mise en page changée ? »), never written empty.
- **Fixtures are INVENTED, not an extract of the real pages** (the lot asked for « un extrait réel de deux points et une route »): the code repository is public and `frame/aerodb/test/fixtures/SOURCES.md`
  forbids another State's text there (each State's copyright; no Irish licence found). They carry the real markup with invented names and coordinates (`TSTAA`, `Z999`). The real pages were read on the
  workstation only (167 points, 31 segments, 10 routes, no limit missing).
- **The cycle in force of the zone** is the edition date (`2026-10-29`, an AIRAC date); a NIL cycle (24 Dec 2026) keeps the previous edition's folder, which is exactly its folder name on the site.

### Spain: the launcher fetches alone; the charts

- **The fetch is the one already there** (`aero_fetch.enaire`, K54): the Airspace and EnRoute sets named on the page are fetched at the current amendment (the page lists the set in force; an
  amendment already present is not fetched again, the previous one is removed); ENAIRE stays a personal-licence source. The cycle is read in the file (see above).
- **`chartsUrl(icao)`** gives Features SOG the address of the aerodrome page: France (eAIP of the SIA, `FR-AD-2.<ICAO>-fr-FR.html`), Spain (`LE…`, `GC…`: patterns checked on 5 Nov 2026 for LEAM, LEMD, GCLP;
  a code the AIP does not have gives a page that opens nothing), Ireland (AirNav's AD 2 page of the published edition of the zone `ie`; nothing without it), null elsewhere (K72). It points to the page that
  lists the charts rather than to one PDF: the names (`LE_AD_2_<ICAO>_<TYPE>_<N>_en.pdf`, `N` a sequence number) are not predictable.

### The raw SIA files leave the root of `data/sia/` (DATA_MAP § 6.2: yes, D4 may move them)

- **Why**: that folder mixed the previous cycle's raw sources with the current base — the user's « toujours périmé ». Now `data/local/sources/sia/<cycle>/` (K73, K74), never tracked, never published.
- **How it stays safe**: the build **reads the new place and falls back on the old** (the move is not a prerequisite, so nothing breaks before it is done); `base/move-sources` copies, checks the
  SHA-256, removes the old except a git-tracked file, writes a manifest; `undo` reads it backwards and never overwrites; a different duplicate is a conflict, said; **nothing is purged**. The
  « kept for 2 cycles, older ones in `_archives/` » of the DATA_MAP is **not** implemented (it would be a purge): to decide.
- **What changes for the user**: « Prendre le fichier téléchargé » now writes the SIA zip's files under `data/local/sources/sia/<cycle>/`, not at the root of `data/sia/`; the build result says what it was built with
  (`builtWith`, `previous`, `siaFrom`); « Purger » of France also removes the new folder's files (the user's own action). To undo: `base/move-sources { undo: true, dry: false }`, and `PICKUP['sia']['to']`
  back to `'sia'`. A file whose date cannot be read has no cycle and is not placed.

### Little Navmap files are no longer followed by themselves (SOG-0023, § 10.35)

- **What it was**: `aerodb.js` loaded `lnmperf.js` and called `start()` 1.5 s after the page on a workstation served by the launcher (every 2 minutes, after every frame synchronisation) and setting the
  folder started it too: sheets and `.lnmperf` files were compared both ways all the time — a plausible cause of the sheets « remises à jour sans cesse ».
- **Now**: `create({ lnmperf })` is `false` by default; setting the folder starts nothing; `api.lnmperf()`, the Settings card (« Synchroniser maintenant » = one explicit pass), `download` and the Aircraft
  module's import and export stay. The card's text says it (4 languages). K75.
- **For a workstation that relied on it**: a sheet changed in Little Navmap no longer reaches the JSON sheet by itself, and a JSON sheet no longer rewrites its `.lnmperf`: import through the Aircraft
  module's preview, or export, or press « Synchroniser maintenant ». To undo: `lnmperf: true` in the call to `AeroDB.create` (and the `start()` in `setFolder`).

## 6 November 2026 — SOG-0038: the headless browsers of the builds are never left running

- **What was seen**: on the morning of 6 Nov, 108 headless browser processes (profiles `%TEMP%\sog-base-<id>`) and 54 profile folders, the leftovers of 54 builds started on 5 Nov
  between 18:00 and 19:56, during the noreg passes. They were killed by hand.
- **Cause (reproduced)**: not the job's end-of-task code — `build()` kills the browser tree and removes the profile in a `finally`. The browser survives when the **launcher process
  itself is stopped by force while a job runs**: on Windows `Popen.terminate()` is `TerminateProcess`, no `finally` runs, and the browser (a plain child, nothing ties it to its
  parent) goes on for ever — its page never exits by itself. The real-launcher cases (K50, with its 150 s wait; K51 through its own browsers) did it every time a run timed out, failed
  an assertion before the job ended, or was stopped by the runner's own timeout under load — and a runner that is itself killed leaves its K51 browsers too. K77 reproduces it on
  `eef653ffb` (ten browser processes left after the launcher is terminated in the middle of a build). The ordinary paths (end, failure, Stop, timeout) were already clean.
- **Now**: (1) the browser is bound to its launcher by the system: on Windows a job object with `JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE`, the process created suspended, assigned, then resumed, so
  that no child escapes before the assignment; the OS closes the handle when the launcher dies, however it dies; `kill_tree` also closes it. Elsewhere the process group is still
  killed by `kill_tree` (a launcher killed there leaves the browser: the sweep below covers it). If the binding fails the process is resumed anyway and works as before. K51 starts its
  browsers the same way, so a stopped runner leaves none. (2) At load, `purge_stale` removes the `sog-base-*` folders older than an hour **and stops the headless browsers whose command line
  holds one of them** (found with PowerShell / `ps`, only when a stale folder exists; `--headless` and the exact folder name are required), and says it on the launcher's output. A profile younger
  than an hour is a job that may be running: untouched. (3) `build()` says in the job log when a profile could not be removed.
- **To undo**: `launch_browser(…)` back to `subprocess.Popen(…)` in `build()`; the sweep is independent.

## 6 November 2026 — SOG-0044: OKASI 2A (LFPO) and the missing approaches of LFLL

- **What the user said**: « Vérifie OKASI2A sur les SID LFPO. il semble qu'elle ne fasse pas partie de la liste, et pourtant elle existe (piste 20). Sur LFLL : beaucoup d'approches manquantes, dont la ILS 17L avec 3 points de transition. »
- **LFPO / OKASI 2A**: there is no OKASI 2A. The AIP in force (eAIP 29 OCT 2026, read online on 6 Nov) names OKASI **2Y** and OKASI **2S** for runway 20 (DATA SID RWY20 CODE_03 and the charts SID RWY20 SOUTH INSTR 01 / 02), and ten OKASI departures in all (06, 07, 20, 24, 25 x two letters); the SIA XML has no SID, the X-Plane CIFP of the workstation holds no LFPO, the previous eAIP is no longer online. The reading holds
  both (status `check`, runway 20): the compiled file `data/proc/manual/LFPO.json` of 6 Nov (08:01) has the 180 SID of the AIP; the current reader reads every LFPO page (K80). The name « 2A » comes from another data set (SimBrief / Navigraph / another cycle) or from memory: to ask the user where it was seen. Nothing to correct for this SID.
- **LFLL**, from the AIP (12 IAC charts): ILS/LOC 17L, 35L, 35R; VOR 17R, 35L, 35R; RNP 17L, 17R, 35L, 35R; INA RNAV 17L-17R and 35L-35R (four transitions each). The SIA publishes **coding tables for the RNP and INA RNAV only** (no table for the ILS and the VOR); DATA 02 gives the FAF of the ILS/LOC (17L, 35L, 35R), the FAF of the RNP (`FL17L`…) and of the VOR.
  The workstation's `data/proc/manual/LFLL.json` (5 Nov, 19:59) held 12 approaches of which 4 were `doubt` (hidden by default: INA RNAV 17L / 17R TALAR and GOMET) and **no** INA RNAV 35L / 35R (page « NON lue : 8 tronçons sur 17 »); the RNP 17L / 17R / 35R had no runway point (« y es » cut), and the reader refused the SID 35L page 02 (26 of 46).
- **Causes, all in the reader** (`tools/proc_sia.py`; see SPEC § 5 quinquies, K79): (1) a 6-cell leg row was not a leg row (threshold 7): it vanished without being counted, the group looked complete and the geometry check (« LL514 → LL510 : 8.00 NM pour 4.0 ») doubted it; (2) a row that lost its « TF » (« LL703 - 315 317.9 4.0 … »); (3) « y es » / « RW17 R y es » / « -1 .0 » / « RNAV 1-- » cut by the extractor; (4) the vertical-angle column took `1.0` (the accuracy), a second valid placement, so the row « TF LL712 - 092 094.7 4.0 - - - - 1.0 » was refused; (5) more than four lost cells; (6) a lone altitude whose column the page does not say (the text layer of the PDF is ONE string per row: no empty cell, no position — verified on the fragments); (7) the SID 35L page 02 prints the fly-over and the turn before the terminator; (8) the point `FL17L` (FAF of the RNP) had no position.
- **What the reader does now**: it reads the rows above, and **does not read the lone altitude** of the INA RNAV 35L / 35R rows (mini or maxi is not said, the same procedure of 17L prints it in the MNM column, but another procedure is not a proof): the legs are there with their point, courses, distance and speed, the altitude is `null`, a warning says it. 17L, 17R, 35L, 35R: 4 RNP and 8 INA RNAV, all `check`, none `doubt`; the SID page 35L / 02 is read (44 of 46 rows; BELUS8N and ROMAM8N keep their first leg unread, 2 `doubt`).
  Rows counted for the first time also improve LFPG (9 departures gain their first leg, e.g. LANVI 6G 09L `CF PG091`), LFPO (`PO6E` 26L) and LFST (INA RNAV 05 IZPIQ): no procedure of LFPG, LFPO, LFBO, LFST, LFRS lost a leg or changed status (checked old reader against new on the cached PDF of 29 Oct).
- **ILS 17L, 35L, 35R of Lyon — decision**: the final only. `ILS RWY 17L` = `FAF17L` (position of the DATA page) → `RW17L` (threshold of AD 2.12), `check`, synthetic points, a warning: no initial fix, no missed approach, **no transition** — the « 3 points de transition » of the ILS 17L are on the IAC chart only, whose text layer is a glyph-name encoding (`/MT65…`) and whose geometry is drawn: reading them is a chart reader (another work item, to decide). Nothing is invented: no VOR final (the FAF of a VOR is not on the runway axis, no ILS 17R: the SIA publishes a VOR there).
- **To undo**: the final-only ILS is the loop « une piste dont le SIA donne le FAF ILS/LOC … » of `proc_sia.build_aerodrome` and `proc_records.final_only_record`; the partial reading of a lone altitude is `_fit_geometry` (`_leg`). A user re-reads LFLL from the Données module (« Lire » / eaip-build): the new reading replaces the previous `eaip` one (`replace_source`), compiled data are never purged.
- **Open**: a chart reader for the transitions of conventional approaches (ILS, VOR) of Lyon; the first legs of BELUS8N / ROMAM8N (SID 35L / 02: nine cells for ten, the placement is ambiguous); to ask the user where « OKASI2A » comes from.

## 6 November 2026 — SOG-0049: all the pages of a family, and « OKASI 2A » again

- **What the user said**: « OKASI 2A - LFPO OKAS2A - OKASI et PILUL sont sur la seconde page de SID RNAV pour la RWY 20. c'est, du coup un cap général : il peut y avoir plusieurs pages ».
- **The check (eAIP 29 OCT 2026, SIA)**: the SID RNAV of runway 20 is a family of **five** coding-table PDFs (`AD_2_LFPO_DATA_SID_RWY20_RNAV_CODE_01 … _05`), and two chart pages (`SID_RWY20_RNAV_SOUTH_INSTR_01` = the « Y » departures, `…_02` = the « S » ones). OKASI and PILUL are on **CODE_03**
  (`OKASI 2Y`, `PILUL 2Y`, `OKASI 2S`, `PILUL 2S`) and on the chart pages SOUTH 01 and 02 (« OKASI 2Y FL 080 … To ODEBU then to OKASI », « OKASI 2S … »). The reading of 13:30 had them (K80 was not green by mistake: it asserts these four names and their legs). **There is no « 2A »** in the AIP in force:
  no LFPO departure ends with the letter A (suffixes 1E 1S 1W 1Y 2E 2F 2G 2H 2Q 2R 2S 2W 2X 2Y); the exact difference is therefore « 2Y and 2S on the chart and in the table, 2A nowhere ». The second page the user sees is the second chart (SOUTH 02 = « S ») or the CODE_03 table.
- **The general rule behind it** (the reader's gap that was real): `read_aerodrome` read the **first page** of each PDF only (`first_page_rows`). The SIA publishes one page per PDF today (checked: the 210 cached PDFs of the cycle, all single-page), so nothing was lost this cycle — but a coding table that ran over
  a second page of a PDF would have been cut without a word. Now every page of every PDF is read (`proc_pdf.all_pages_rows`, `build_aerodrome(pages_of=…)`), each counted on its own, and the reading reports **per family of pages** how many it cited, read, found unreadable, and which numbers of the series are missing (SPEC § 5 quinquies).
- **Effect, cold, on the cycle in force** (the cached PDFs, nothing written in data/): LFPO 60 pages read of 60 (31 families + DATA), LFPG 90/90 (39), LFLL 21/21 (15), LFBO 21/21 (17); no unread page, no missing number; the procedures are **identical** to the reading before (same ids, same status, same legs: LFPO 309, LFPG 711, LFLL 81, LFBO 78). So nothing is gained on this cycle — the gain is the guarantee and the count.
- **What it does not do**: it does not read the charts. A SID printed on a chart and in **no** table (a page the AIP does not cite) is not detected; a cross-check of the names of the charts against the tables would need the ~100 chart PDFs of an aerodrome (work item, to decide).
- **Fixtures**: a set keeps one page per PDF; `tools/make_eaip_fixtures.py` now refuses a PDF of several pages (it would cut it); the multi-page case is proved with a PDF built in the test (K81).
- **To undo**: `proc_sia.read_aerodrome` (`pages_of`) and the loop of `build_aerodrome`; the families are `proc_sia.page_families` / `families_line`.

## 6 November 2026 — SOG-0045: `AeroDb.chartUrl(proc)`

- **Design**: V3_SPECIFICATION § 10.39. Fuel's Procédures tab and the in-flight step ask for one « Carte » link per chosen procedure (shape fixed by Features SOG in `modules/fuel/INTEGRATION.md`, « Charts through aerodb »); null keeps the aerodrome page.
- **What the data allows** (checked on the three files of the workstation, LFPO, LFLL, LFBO): an approach's chart is found from names — the DATA page of its table and its IAC chart share the runway and the words (`FNA_ILS_Z_LOC_Z`, `FNA_RNP`; LFLL `RWY17L_17R_INA_RNAV` covers two runways). A SID or STAR chart is **not** findable from names in general: LFPO has five charts for the SID RNAV of runway 20 (`EAST`, `NORTH`, `SOUTH_INSTR_01`, `_02`, `WEST`) and the file names do not say which departures each carries (OKASI 2Y is on `SOUTH_INSTR_01`, OKASI 2S on `_02`: that is in the drawing).
  Only a few charts carry the names in the file name (`SID_RWY06-07_RNAV_OLZOM_MONOT_DORDI`, the STAR entry-point charts). So the rule is « a chart only when exactly one matches, else the DATA page »: the DATA page is the table the record was read from, always the right page for a procedure, never a wrong chart.
- **Not done, and why**: for SID / STAR the match through the name stays rare. Making it general needs the names printed on each chart (a reader of the chart text, ~100 PDF per aerodrome, `SOUTH_INSTR_01` → « OKASI 2Y … PILUL 2Y ») to be added to `charts.items[].names` by the reader — a work item, to decide. INA RNAV approaches (charts named `RWY_EAST` / `RWY_WEST` + entry point) are not matched either: the direction-to-runway link is not in the names.
- **Safety**: the file of an aerodrome is in the synchronised repository, writable by any member of the group: the base must be under the SIA host (as for `source.url`), the path of a chart must be `Cartes/<ICAO>/<plain name>.pdf` of the same aerodrome and listed by the file itself, the VAC address must be a SIA pdf. Nothing else becomes a link.
- **Test**: noreg K83 (approach, SID with a name chart, SID without one → DATA page, STAR, runway → ADC / VAC, ambiguity → DATA page, other State / OFP / junk → null, a file with a foreign base → null); red before (`db.chartUrl is not a function`).

## 6 November 2026 — SOG-0046: the ground layer, the file side (V3 § 10.40)

- **What**: the launcher job `ground/fetch` (`tools/regen/ground.py`, same family as the base jobs: dry run, manifest, one aerodrome per call, MIN_GAP 30 s) asks Overpass ONCE for the taxiways, stands, gates and holding points inside the aerodrome area tagged `icao=<ICAO>` and writes `data/proc/ground/<ICAO>.json`
  (`{ icao, cycle: <day>, source: 'osm', attribution: '© OpenStreetMap contributors, ODbL', items }`, compacted: LFPO about 40 kB) and the manifest `proc/ground/index.json`, then ONE commit (no push). `AeroDb.ground(icao)` reads it, `AeroDb.fetchGround(icao)` is the page's gesture (SPEC, API table).
- **Licence (user's yes, 6 Nov 2026, 15:50)**: the file is a derived database under the ODbL; the data repository is the airline's private repository (not a public distribution); the attribution travels in the file and is shown by the map while the layer is drawn. `apt.dat` is not a source (nothing read from the simulator).
- **Bounds**: names of 16 signs, 5 000 items (taxiways first, then stands, then holding points: a cap drops the least useful), 2 000 points a line, coordinates rounded to 1e-6, one-point taxiways, relations, duplicate ids and invalid coordinates dropped and said; nothing is written without an item (`no-data`). The frame hands the map a bounded copy again: `proc/ground/` is an untrusted input like every file of `data/`.
- **A stand drawn as a polygon is its centre** (mean of the vertices); a taxiway keeps its line; a holding point drawn as a way is its centre too.
- **Not mine**: the item « ground » of the Données inventory is in `modules/data` (Features / BUGS SOG): it can call `AeroDb.fetchGround(icao)` and read `proc/ground/index.json` (cycle and counts per aerodrome); the drawing and the panel are BUGS SOG's.
- **Test**: noreg K84 (the job on a canned Overpass answer, never the public instance: dry run, one request, the file, the bounds, no-data, HTTP 429, an answer that is not JSON) and K85 (`ground()` as an untrusted input, `fetchGround` through its reasons); red before (the job and the functions did not exist). The answer of the test is synthetic (`frame/aerodb/test/fixtures/ground/`), not a recording: a first real read of LFPO (about 30 s) is the pilot's gesture on the workstation.
- **To undo**: `tools/regen/ground.py` (delete the file: the verb disappears) and the two functions of `aerodb.js`; the files already written stay (compiled data are never purged).

## 6 November 2026 — SOG-0054: the SIA's overseas eAIP (survey, nothing coded)

- **What the user said**: « NTAA : fait partie de la polynésie française, les cartes sont donc publiées par le SIA. il y a d'autres AD d'îles publiées par le SIA, NWWM par ex. »
- **The SIA publishes six eAIP in one disc** (`/media/dvd/eAIP_29_OCT_2026/`, home page `index.html`, shown at `/documents/htmlshow?f=dvd/eAIP_29_OCT_2026/<REGION>/home.html`): `FRANCE` (metropolitan, what the suite reads), `PAC-P` (French Polynesia), `PAC-N` (New Caledonia and Wallis), `RUN` (Réunion, Mayotte), `CAR-SAM-NAM` (Antilles, Guyane, Saint-Pierre-et-Miquelon…) and `Atlas-VAC`. The home pages of the regions are empty stubs (the issue list is loaded by script).
- **Same address scheme, other folder**: `<disc>/<REGION>/AIRAC-<date>/html/eAIP/FR-menu-fr-FR.html` (menu), `FR-AD-2.<ICAO>-fr-FR.html` (aerodrome page), `Cartes/<ICAO>/AD_2_<ICAO>_DATA_…_CODE.pdf` (coding tables): exactly the metropolitan layout. **The `<date>` is the region's own AIRAC and is not the disc's**: on the 29 Oct disc, `PAC-P` and `PAC-N` are `AIRAC-2026-10-29`, `RUN` and `CAR-SAM-NAM` are `AIRAC-2026-10-01` (the 28-day calendar, one cycle back: the regional issues are not republished each cycle), so the address of a region is found by trying the cycle dates back from the disc's (what this survey did, up to 14), or from the issue list of the home page (script-loaded: not read).
  `proc_sia.eaip_url(cycle)` and `catalogue.js eaipBase(cycle)` compose only `<disc dated by the cycle>/FRANCE/AIRAC-<cycle>`: they would need a region and a region cycle.
- **Aerodromes** (read from the menus): PAC-P 49 (NTAA Faa'a, NTTB Bora-Bora, NTGA, NTAR, NTTM, the Tuamotu / Marquises / Australes airfields…), PAC-N 15 (NWWW La Tontouta, NWWM Nouméa-Magenta, NWWL, NWWK, NWWV, NLWW Wallis Hihifo, NLWF Futuna…), RUN 3 (FMEE, FMEP, FMCZ), CAR-SAM-NAM 19 (TFFF, TFFR, TFFJ, TFFG, TFFS, TFFA…, SOCA Cayenne, SOOA…, LFVP Saint-Pierre, LFVM Miquelon). The ICAO prefixes are not `LF` (NT, NW, NL, FM, TF, SO, with LFVP / LFVM): the suite's « France = LF » test does not cover them (`proc/eaip-fetch`, `chartsUrl`, `chartUrl`, the zone `fr`).
- **The coding tables are the same format, read by the same reader** (cold, in a temporary folder, nothing written in `data/`; `read_aerodrome` with the regional folder as `eaip`): NTAA 21 procedures (12 SID, 4 STAR, 5 approaches, 2 of them « doubt »), 7 pages of 7 read; FMEE 79 (46 SID, 19 STAR, 14 approaches), 14 of 15 pages; TFFF 56, 7 of 8 pages; NWWW 0 (its two RNP pages, `…_CODE_RNP.pdf` and `…RNP_CODE.pdf`, are not recognised: 1 page of 3 read); NWWM has no coding table (the AIP gives charts only: nothing to read, the chart address of `chartUrl` would still work with a regional base). The unread pages are ILS Y / Z and RNP Y / Z variants (`DATA_RWY14_ILS_Y_CODE`, `…ILS_Z_LOC_Z_CODE`, `…RNP_CODE_RNP`): a layout variant to look at, not a format break. Families and page counts are said by the reader (SOG-0049).
- **Not checked**: the SIA XML data (aerodromes, navaids, points of the base, zone `fr`): whether the SIA publishes an overseas base is not known; the base resolver (`base_resolver`) places the en-route points of the tables from it, so without it the points are placed by the page DATA or by the reading (openAIP).
- **Not coded**: the zones `pf` / `nc` (or an « SIA overseas » zone) as dated sources need (1) a region + region-cycle address in `proc_sia.py` / `catalogue.js`, (2) the ICAO-prefix → region table (NT → PAC-P, NW and NL → PAC-N, FM → RUN, TF, SO, LFVP, LFVM → CAR-SAM-NAM; to be checked against the menus), (3) the reader's rule for « France » (`LF`) opened to those prefixes in `proc/eaip-fetch`, `chartsUrl` and `chartUrl`, (4) the base question above. About a day of work with its cases; Monday.

## 9 November 2026 — SOG-0064: China and Nicosia are not in the list of downloadable packages

- **What the user said**: « Chine et Nicosie ne sont pas dans la liste des packages téléchargeables (bdd aéro) ».
- **Why**: the list of downloadable packages is the list of **published** zones (`aerodb.zones()` of the publication: one `sia/base/<zone>/` per zone built by a workstation and pushed to the data repository). On the workstation of 6 Nov the published zones are af, be, ch, de, dz, es, fr, gb, id, ie, in, iq, ir, it, kh, ma, mm, nat, nl, pk, pt, sy, th, tr, us, vn: **cn (China) and cy (Cyprus, the Nicosia FIR) were never built**, nothing more. No zone, source or licence is missing.
- **What exists** (checked 9 Nov): both zones are in the catalogue (`shared/carto/aero_zones_geo.js`, names in four languages; they are in « Ajouter un pays »); the build job accepts them; their source is **openAIP**, which exports the country (`cn_asp` 857 kB, `cn_apt` 278 kB, `cn_nav` 42 kB; `cy_asp` 192 kB, `cy_apt` 17 kB, `cy_nav` 12 kB; no `rpp` layer for either, which the downloader reports as absent, not as a failure), licence CC BY-NC 4.0 (© openAIP) like every openAIP zone. No official source: the SIA, ENAIRE and AirNav do not cover them; China's and Cyprus's AIP are not read by the suite.
- **Not covered**: Northern Cyprus (`cyn`, a zone of its own in the catalogue, outline of the Turkish Republic of Northern Cyprus): openAIP has no export for it (404 on every layer, 9 Nov), so it stays empty; the Nicosia FIR is served by the `cy` zone for the Republic. Taiwan (`tw`) has an aerodrome layer only, Hong Kong airspace and aerodromes: they would give partial bases.
- **To add them**: the pilot's gesture on the workstation, in Données: « Ajouter un pays » → China, Cyprus → Download (openAIP, 30 s between two downloads) → Build → the push of the data repository: the base of each zone is written in `data/sia/base/`. Not done here (the data repository is never written by this session): the case K86 proves that nothing in the code stands in the way (catalogue, sources, build job, manifest).
- **Test**: noreg K86 (green before and after: there was no defect; it keeps the two zones buildable).

## 9 November 2026 — SOG-0052 step 2, the launcher side: the Output folder of X-Plane (job `stats/output`)

- **What**: `tools/regen/stats.py`, verb `stats/output` `{ action, folder?, name? }` (this workstation only, like every job). Called by a page as `launcher.job('regen/job', { set: 'stats', verb: 'output', args: { action: 'status' } })`.
  `status` → `{ ok, set, exists, label, files: [{ name, size, mtime }] }`; `set { folder }` (a path typed or chosen by the user) → the same; `clear`; `read { name }` → `{ ok, name, size, _text }` or `{ ok: false, reason: 'no-folder' | 'no-file' | 'too-large' | 'unreadable' }`.
  The page gives `result._text` to `modules/stats/phases.js` (`parseWindows`): the contract of the module is unchanged (the text, bounded to 64 MB).
- **Rules** (§ 10.37 A): X-Plane stays LOCAL. The setting is `data/local/xp_output.json` (`local/`: never versioned, never published); the **full path is never given back** (`label`: the last two names, « X-Plane 12/Output »); the data folder, the suite folder and their parents are refused;
  a journal is a bare name `FuelPhaseMonitor*.csv` or `.csv.gz` (no separator, no `..`, no symbolic link, a regular file inside the folder); 64 MB at most, a `.gz` decompressed under the same bound.
- **The samples are never written**: the text travels in the key `_text` of the result and **a result key starting with `_` is not kept by the launcher** in its last-job file (`regen_jobs.Jobs._save`: the file would have held a copy of the user's flight data). The text stays in the launcher's memory until the next job.
- **Not mine, to do on the page side** (`modules/aircraft/sheets.js`, Features SOG): the « Depuis le dossier Output » choice next to the file picker (status → the list, `read` → `parseWindows`), the setting of the folder, the detection of the route from the journal. No frame function was added: the page uses `launcher.job` as for `fetchProcedures`.
- **Test**: noreg K87 (arguments, listing, set refusals, label without the path, read of a plain and a gzipped journal, the bound, a link, the last-job file without the text: red before on the old `_save`).

## 9 November 2026 — SOG-0054: the SIA's overseas eAIP, read like France (no zone per region)

- **What was asked** (Simplification, from the survey of 6 Nov): dated zones per overseas region (pf, nc, re, ag…), same readers, one case per region. **What was done instead, and why**: no new zone. The zone `fr` already holds the overseas territories (Natural Earth outlines grouped under `fr` in `tools/build_aero_zones.py`, and the SIA's raw file is `AIXM4.5_all_FR_OM_<date>.xml`: France **and Overseas**): the base of 29 Oct 2026 on the workstation contains NTAA, NWWM, FMEE, TFFF and LFVP among its 1 872 aerodromes. Four more zones would split one source in five, give each a cycle it does not have in the XML, and break the `fr` outline (and `ag` is Antigua and Barbuda in ISO). What was missing is the rest of the chain: the **addresses and the reading** of the procedures, which stopped at `LF`.
- **What the code does now**:
  - `proc_sia.SIA_REGIONS` / `region_of(icao)` (mirrored by `AeroCatalogue.SIA_REGIONS` / `siaRegionOf`, case K89): FRANCE (`LF`), PAC-P (`NT`), PAC-N (`NW`, `NL`), RUN (`FMCZ`, `FMEE`, `FMEP` exactly: the other `FM` terrains are Madagascar's and the Comoros'), CAR-SAM-NAM (`TF`, `SO`, and `LFVM`, `LFVP` exactly: Saint-Pierre-et-Miquelon, which an `LF` test sent to France). The exact codes come first.
  - `region_eaip(cycle, region)`: the address of a region's eAIP = the disc of the cycle (`eAIP_29_OCT_2026`) + the region + **the region's own date**, which is written in its home page (`init('RUN','2026','10','01','10')`) and is not on the calendar (on 29 Oct: PAC-P and PAC-N 2026-10-29, RUN and CAR-SAM-NAM 2026-10-01). One request per region; FRANCE needs none.
  - `proc/eaip-fetch` accepts these terrains (and `proc/ead-fetch` refuses them: the SIA is never read through EAD); `_read_eaip` reads the aerodrome page of the region with the same reader and the same coding tables (the survey read NTAA 21 procedures, FMEE 79, TFFF 56 with the unchanged reader). **The cycle written in the file is the disc's** (the calendar cycle in force), not the region's folder date, so « outdated » keeps meaning « a later disc exists »; `charts.base` keeps the region's real folder, which `chartUrl` uses.
  - `proc/regions` `{}`: one request per region, writes `data/proc/sia-regions.json` (`{ schema, cycle, updated_at, regions: { <REGION>: { date, base } } }`) and one commit; aerodb's `chartsUrl` reads it for the pages of overseas terrains (null without it: a date is never guessed). `chartUrl` needs no file of regions: it uses the `charts.base` of the aerodrome's own file (checked under the SIA host). `coverage()` and `fetchProcedures` treat these terrains like France (« sia », verb `eaip-fetch`).
- **Not done**: the per-State bulk jobs (`proc/fetch`, `proc/build`) still handle `LF` only (the metropolitan menu); the 15–50 overseas aerodromes are read on demand, one at a time, which is the pilot's gesture anyway. The two NWWW RNP pages (`…_CODE_RNP.pdf`) and the ILS / RNP Y-Z variants that the survey found unread stay unread (their layout is to look at: a work item). The Données screen has no line for the regions yet (`proc/regions` is a verb to call: `launcher.job('regen/job', { set: 'proc', verb: 'regions', args: {} })`).
- **Test**: noreg K88 (the launcher: region of each terrain, the home page of each region, the address under its own date, the jobs, one block per region) and K89 (aerodb: the mirror of the table, `chartsUrl`, coverage, the verb, `chartUrl`, a hostile base): red before (none of it existed).

## 9 November 2026 — SOG-0030, last item: `base/move-sources` under the synchronisation of data/

- A real move writes in `data/` (copy, SHA-256 check, removal of the old copy, manifest) while a synchronisation of the data repository may be rewriting it. The launcher now gives its jobs the synchronisation lock (`ctx['sync_lock']` = `SYNC_LOCK` of `tools/launch.py`); a **real** `base/move-sources` (move or undo) takes it **without waiting**, answers `{ ok: false, reason: 'syncing', message }` and touches nothing when a synchronisation holds it, and keeps it for the whole move so that no synchronisation starts in the middle. The plan (`dry`, the default) writes nothing and answers under the lock. A context without the lock (an older launcher, a test) moves as before. The Données screen already refuses while a merge is pending or an operation runs; it will show the new reason as a refusal (`syncing`) — to word on its side.
- **Test**: noreg K90 (refused under the lock, the undo too, the plan answers, the lock is held during the move and given back, no lock in the context: as before); red before (the move went ahead under the lock).

## 9 November 2026 — SOG-0028 (5): the .lnmperf cruise comes from the sheet's table

- Features SOG wrote the patch (`lnmCruiseFromCurve`), AeroDB applied it to `frame/aerodb/sheets.js`. `toLnmperf` writes as cruise flow (and TAS) the value of the sheet's `rpmFuelCurve` at the cruise rpm (`cruise.rpmNominal`) on the curve of the altitude closest to `cruise.refAltFt`: the row itself, or a linear interpolation between the two numbered rows that frame the rpm (the rule of Fuel's `tableAt`); no rpm, no reference altitude, no framing rows: the sheet's `cruise` as before. It runs after the kg → lb conversion, which converts the table with the rest. Rows without a TAS leave the sheet's TAS.
- **Import unchanged**: a `.lnmperf` has neither rpm nor altitude, so `parseLnmperf` creates no table row (the sheet's table stays the single source, § 10.35). Reviewed: unit handling checked (the conversion covers every `fuelFlowPerHour` of the table), the closest-altitude choice and the interpolation have a case each.
- **Test**: noreg K91 (between two rows, a row at the rpm, the closest altitude, the five fall-backs, rows without TAS, a sheet in kg, strings read as numbers, the import's no-row rule); red before (the function did not exist).

## 9 November 2026 — The registry follows the current airline (§ 10.38 step 1, rest signalled by Features SOG)

- **Defect**: the registry (`registry.js`, `PATHS.fleet`) read `airline/fleet.json` only; after the migration (`airline/<code>/fleet.json`) its search, `fleetTypes` (the « to complete » types) and `aircraftOf` no longer saw the fleet.
- **Now**: `aircraft()` asks `FrameData.airline()` for the folder (`root`) and reads `<root>fleet.json` — `AeroDBRegistry.fleetPathOf(root)` accepts only the three shapes the frame gives (`airline/`, `airline/<code>/` with a code of 2 to 8 lower-case signs, `pilot/<trigram>/airline/perso/`); any other folder, no folder, or a frame without `airline()` reads `airline/fleet.json` as before (no path is ever invented from an entry). The registry lists the airline's folder (not only `airline/`) to know whether the fleet file exists. A change of airline (the window event `frame-data-airline`, and the personal fleet file under `pilot/`) drops the registry, which is read again at the next question and announces `{ aircraft: true }`; a change during a read marks the read registry as stale.
- **The fleet is an untrusted input** (`cleanFleet`, the same bounds as Fuel's reader): 200 aircraft at most, registration 2 to 10 signs `[A-Z0-9-]` (otherwise the aircraft is left), type 2 to 4 signs (otherwise empty: the aircraft stays), callsigns of 12 signs, other texts of 40 signs without control signs, unknown fields not copied, a registration once.
- **Test**: noreg K92 (no frame: as before; the default airline; a switch to `airline/co2/` and back; the personal folder; ten folders no airline can have; a file of 300 aircraft and its bad rows; the announcement); red before. Existing cases F79, F103, F106, M1, M2, M32, N2, N7, N8, K62 unchanged.

ENAIRE (es) and AirNav (ie) follow the same rule (same reader, same gate); es and ie published **no** airways either, for the same reason as far as the files go (no ENAIRE file in `data/sia/aixm5`, no `zones/ie` on this workstation: not checked further).

## 10 November 2026 — SOG-0081: the route calculator behind two launcher jobs (alpha.5)

- **The user's words**: « l'algo de calcul est à intégrer dans le calcul des routes […] le frein majeur est sur la première tâche du pilotage : le choix de la route, calcul auto, et respect des règles IFPS ingérables. Les fichiers excel source, en provenance d'eurocontrol, sont dans downloads, ça doit partir en α-5. ça va encore bouger au niveau de l'algo […] tu feras un diff sur le calcul en début d'A.M. »
- **Engine**: `tools/route_engine/` = RouteSim 2's `engine.py`, `fra.py`, `procedures.py`, `flight_profile.py` copied **without change of substance** (relative imports and three path variables only, every changed line commented `SOG-0081`): the user's diffs apply as they are (README of the package). `plan.py` ports `server.plan()` (the calculation, the control, up to three alternatives); `server.py` and `web/` are not taken (the screen is Fuel's).
- **Two jobs** (`tools/regen/route.py`, set `route`): `route/compute` (closed request, bounded plan; contract in INTEGRATION « Route computation », sent to Features SOG) and `route/import-rad` (plan → pick → compile, like `base/pickup`). The page never computes. Nothing is cached between two computes (the base, the procedures and the RAD change under the launcher; about 2 s of reading is the price of never serving stale data).
- **No openpyxl** (RouteSim 2's importer used it): the launcher's rule is the standard library (CLAUDE.md lists the only declared exceptions, pypdf and the OCR), so `route_engine/xlsx.py` reads the workbooks with `zipfile` + `xml.etree` (DOCTYPE refused, sizes and counts bounded). Checked on the workstation against RouteSim 2's compiled files: the two RAD workbooks (`RAD_2611_v1_23`, `RAD_2612_v1_6`) and the four FRA workbooks (cycles 2609–2612) give **exactly** the books RouteSim 2 holds (content equal, SHA-256 included; struck text removed as with openpyxl: the style of a cell, or a struck run of a rich text).
- **Data**: `data/route/rad.json.gz` (2.1 MB) and `fra.json.gz` (0.7 MB), compiled data, **published** (a new output of the launcher: `route/` joins `OUTPUTS`), never purged (the same cycle is replaced, the others stay), the cycle of the workbook (`Valid From` of the RAD cover, `Effective Date` of the FRA cover); the workbooks are not copied (licence). The engine reads them from there, no longer from `rad/`.
- **Licence** of the workbooks: EUROCONTROL Network Manager documents (the RAD is a public document of the Network Manager, the FRA points file is published with each AIRAC); the reuse terms are not established: use by the company, published in its **private** repository only; noted in the catalogue (`eurocontrol_rad`, Données › Sources, ×4 languages) and in the verb's documentation.
- **What the engine says it does not do** (kept visible in `gaps` and `limitation`): the full RAD grammar, conditional ceilings, complex times, crossed airspaces, FRA / NPZ volumes, EAUP / EUUP activations and NOTAM; « no incompatibility detected » covers only `checked`. It is a simulation precheck, not an IFPS validation.
- **Not done here**: the direction of the airways (the airways files give none: `direction: 'inconnue'`, SOG-0078), the screen (Features SOG), the FRA rules in evolution (the user's diff).
- **Tests**: noreg K101 (the workbook reader and the compiled books), K102 (`route/import-rad`: plan, pick, changed file refused, merge by cycle, one commit), K103 (`route/compute`: the example route, the mandatory points, a RAD segment rule that makes the plan impossible, the closed request, the bounds).

## 10 November 2026 — SOG-0078: the direction of the airways was in the sources and never kept

- **The question**: « il semble que les AWY à sens unique ne soient pas cartographiées dans aerodb ? » — and RouteSim's README: « les fichiers airways AeroDB ne fournissent pas de sens ». True of the **published files**, but the sources DO say it.
- **What the sources say**: SIA (AIXM 4.5): each segment `Rsg` has usages `Rsu` with `codeDir` `F` (start → end) or `B` (end → start) and `codeRteAvbl` (`NONFUA`, `CDR1`…): a two-way segment has an `F` usage and a `B` usage (the level rules by direction hang on them), a one-way one a single usage. NATS / ENAIRE (AIXM 5.1): `RouteAvailability` `direction` `FORWARD` / `BACKWARD` / `BOTH` (with `status` `OPEN`, `COND`, `CLOSED`). X-Plane `earth_awy.dat`: `N` / `F` / `B`. AirNav Ireland (ENR 3.3 geojson): nothing. **No reader kept it**, so the published airways had none and the engine assumed both ways.
- **Measured on the files of the workstation** (what a rebuild will publish): `fr` (1 493 segments): **334 forward only, 317 backward only (651 one-way, 44 %)**, 838 both ways, 4 without usage; `gb` (NATS file, 1 217 segments): 459 forward only, 433 backward only, 325 both ways.
- **Now**: `dir` = `F` | `B` | `FB` in every airways record, absent when the source is silent (`shared/carto/aero_symbols.js` for the SIA — permanent usages first, a conditional `CDR` usage only when there is no permanent one —, `shared/aero_sources.js` for AIXM 5.1 — closed availabilities ignored — and X-Plane, kept through `resolveAirways`). **Parser version 22** (`shared/sia_base.js` VERSION, `AeroCatalogue.PARSER_VERSION`): the zones built before have no `dir` and are shown « à reconstruire » by the Données module; **fr and gb must be rebuilt to publish the direction** (nothing was rebuilt or written here).
- **The engine** (`tools/route_engine/engine.py`, the only change of substance to RouteSim 2's engine, every line commented `SOG-0078`; the user's diff of the same lines will conflict, to settle together): `Nav.graph` flies a `dir: 'F'` segment `a → b` only and `'B'` `b → a` only; both ways stay when `dir` is `FB` or absent (the simulation assumption of RouteSim, said by `direction: 'inconnue'`). The calculation never uses a one-way segment against its direction; the **control** of a route that does says `Sens interdit : <airway> est à sens unique et <A> → <B> le parcourt à contre-sens` (level ERREUR); each leg says `direction`: `sens unique`, `double sens` or `inconnue`.
- **Not mine**: the arrow on the Map (`modules/maps`, BUGS SOG: a segment with `dir` `F` or `B` is one-way, `a` and `b` are the start and end of the segment as the source gives them); `shared/mission_routing.js` (the mission router still takes both ways).
- **Not done**: the level rules by direction (odd / even levels by direction hang on `Rul`, they are not coded: a two-way segment is two-way at every level), the time conditions of the conditional usages.
- **Test**: noreg K104 (the three readers, with the conditional-usage rule) and K105 (the engine: forward, backward, reversed control, both, silent).

## 10 November 2026 — SOG-0081: `route/compute` answers in the shape of the Fuel screen

- **The request** (Features SOG, relayed by Simplification): the screen calls `route/compute` with `{ action: 'compute' | 'check', adep, adest, fl, rwyDep?, rwyArr?, date?, via | route }` and reads `{ ok, alternatives, segments, issues }`. **Adopted**: the engine can render it, all of it comes from what `plan()` already computes (legs with `compatible` and `alerts`, procedures used, profile, issues).
- **Where**: a view over the engine, `tools/route_engine/view.py` (the engine and its port `plan.py` are not touched for it); `route/compute` switches on the presence of `action`, so the engine's own form stays for any other caller (`raw: true` adds it to the screen's answer).
- **What is built, not copied**: `name` of an alternative comes from its legs (« Via N160 · A34 », « Direct FRA », « Direct », level appended when it differs from the asked one); `faults` = segments in error; `kind` of a reason is **read from the engine's text** (`RAD` with `ruleId`, `floor` / `ceiling` with `deltaFt`, `FRA`, `direction`, `continuity`, else `altitude`), because the engine states the reasons as sentences, not codes: a new sentence of the engine that matches none is `altitude`, shown with its text, never dropped; `sid` / `star` carry the AeroDB id of the procedure the engine used.
- **Deviation** (the one thing not as asked): in `check` the engine no longer limits the typed route (`via=[]`): its former behaviour refused a route with more than 8 non-airway points, which a typed route has.
- **Test**: noreg K106 (closed request, kinds, compute and check on a small base with a RAD rule, raw).

## 10 November 2026 (17 h) — SOG-0071: the state per zone and the incremental update in the launcher

- **Asked** (V3 § 10.44): a download or a build downloads or builds everything: one call that says per zone `up-to-date | stale(reason) | never-built | failed | building` in the pilot's words, and the incremental decision of « Mettre à jour » in the launcher.
- **Done**: `tools/regen/base_state.py`, verbs `base/status` and `base/update`; `base/build` keeps, per zone, what it read (`data/local/build/<zone>.json`). The reasons are **codes with parameters**, not French sentences: the launcher has no translation and every interface text is in four languages; the page writes them (INTEGRATION gives the four sentences per code).
- **Decisions**: (1) **`building` is the page's**: the launcher holds one job at a time, a status job cannot run during a build. (2) **A zone without a dated source is never stale by the cycle** (K76 already said never « dépassée »): otherwise every AIRAC would rebuild forty openAIP countries for nothing. (3) **`source-old` and `pickup-waiting` are not buildable** (a build would only rebuild the same cycle): they are said, and « Mettre à jour » does not build them: this is what makes it idempotent. (4) **The update does not take files from Downloads**: the pickup keeps its plan-then-answer (the file shown is the file taken, Sécurisation's rule); the update says which zones wait for it. (5) **`inputs-changed` by hash, from a record kept next to the data** (not in the published meta, which would have changed the compiled files): a file is hashed again only if its size or date moved.
- **Measured on this workstation**: 45 zones built, all `parser-old` (built at parser 21, parser now 22): the first « Mettre à jour » after this lot rebuilds the 45 once (the direction of the airways, SOG-0078); the second does nothing.
- **Not done**: the page (Réglages); `base/status` never says `building` (above); an interrupted build leaves no mark (the zone is judged on its files).
- **Test**: noreg K109, one assertion per state, red before (the verbs did not exist), and the idempotence of the update with a build that writes the folders and the records; K46 (one commit per zone) still green.

## 10 November 2026 (18 h) — SOG-0135: the ground read with the procedures; SOG-0138: the world forecast job

- **SOG-0135** (« on ne va pas créer un bouton en plus »): `fetchProcedures` now reads the ground after the procedures (INTEGRATION). Decisions: the reading is in the background and never changes the procedures' answer (a failed ground is not an error of the procedures); « périmé » = dated before the cycle in force (OpenStreetMap moves slowly, a read per AIRAC cycle is enough; the pilot can still read it by hand); `{ ground: false }` for a caller that does not want it. Chosen: cut each ring at the vertices where the set of countries carrying the edge changes, simplify each arc ONCE (canonical direction, cached: both countries get the same vertices). Not chosen: keeping the original vertices of shared borders (heavier file) or a topology library (standard library only). Natural Earth shares the vertices of two neighbours; where it does not (US/CA: two vertices 0.008° apart) the rings are simplified alone as before. K149.

Conditions: the personal-use box is mandatory (`not-personal` without it), the path of the base never comes from the page, a device of the local network cannot start a job. **Counter-proofs** (audit of 7 Oct, review of 10 Nov; K147): without the box: refused; the request with a path: 400; a local-network device: the jobs route is `poste` (403); the database untouched (size, date, folder listing). Cause: `hidden()` of the launcher never serves `data/local/` (audit of 7 Oct), so no real launcher could give the page its files; the cases played a launcher that serves it. Rejected: a narrow read route (a new surface, and one more per local item: the SOG-0144 fault again). Kept: `hidden()` unchanged; `weather/` ignored by git (`data/.gitignore` and `.git/info/exclude`, put by the verb) and outside the launcher's tree (`FRAME_SKIP`); the old folder moved once. K143 on the real launcher class.

The folder is one constant of `tools/regen/weather.py` if the choice is reversed. The page-side change (`weather_store.js` reading `/data/local/weather/world/` when `/weather/world/` is absent) belongs to the frame. ## 10 November 2026 (22 h) — SOG-0144: « Tout recalculer » fails for every zone (page-error): found, not fixed here

- **Reproduced on the real launcher** (`tools/launch.py --data <a copy of the workstation's inputs>`, the 46 zones of the user's job): **46 × `page-error`, « AIXM4.5_all_FR_OM_2026-10-01.xml : HTTP 404 »**, in 6 s. The same build on the test site of the cases (K49) **succeeds** (46 zones in 14 s; with the « personnel » box declared: 36 s): so the page, the parser 23 and the offer are not at fault.
- **Cause**: the raw SIA XML files were **moved to `data/local/sources/sia/<cycle>/`** (« Déplacer les sources », lot C2 of 5 Nov, run on this workstation after the builds of 9 Nov 09 h), and the build page loads them from `/data/local/sources/sia/<cycle>/<name>`; **the real launcher does not serve `data/local/`** (`tools/launch.py › hidden()`: `parts[:2] == ['data', 'local']`, the rule of the audit of 7 Oct: copy of the browser storage, backups). The first of these files the page asks for answers 404, the page's `fatal` ('*') fails **every** zone of the job. The cases never saw it: their test site served `data/sia/` only, and the launcher's own case (K50) builds an invented zone without SIA.
- **Two ways out, neither applied by me**: (A) **the launcher serves `data/local/sources/` only** (a line in `hidden()`: `parts[2:3] != ['sources']`, the rest of `data/local/` stays hidden; open-licence source data, no secret): it is an **access rule of the launcher** (the audit's), so a decision of Sécurisation / the user, with a case on the real launcher (`/data/local/sources/…` 200, `/data/local/build/…` 404, `..` refused); I tried the edit and **the permission classifier refused it**, so it is not made. (B) **at once, without code**: undo the move with the existing job `base/move-sources` `{ dry: false, undo: true }` (the files go back to `data/sia/`: the builds work as on 9 Nov at 09 h; the plan first: `{ dry: true, undo: true }`).
- **Done here**: the text of a failure is no longer lost (above): `data/local/build/<zone>.json › failed.message`, `base/status` reason `failed.message`, a log line `page : …`. Test: K46 (the record of a zone that failed with « boom ») and K109 (the reason carries the message).

- **Update the same evening** (the user's screenshot, relayed): the refusal they saw, « Le lanceur a refusé la demande (arguments invalides) » for « Sélection : 78 zones », is **`base/build` limited to 60 zones** with a refusal that said nothing: every refusal of the set `base` now says its rule (INTEGRATION), K116; the page cuts into slices of 60. The 404 above is a **second**, independent cause that the slices will meet next (a build of up to 60 zones fails the same way on the real launcher while the SIA XML are in `data/local/sources/`).

- **Fix applied (C), the ergonomics session's decision**: not the exception for `data/local/sources/` in `hidden()` (the audit's rule stays as it is) but **a reading carried by the job**: INTEGRATION. K117 (red before: `XML_SIA_2026-10-29.xml : HTTP 404` on the real launcher): the route's rules on the real `Jobs` (token, offered files only, no `..`), `data/local/` still 404, the route without header 403, and the real launcher + real browser building a zone while the SIA XML are in `data/local/sources/`. The user's 46 zones build on the real launcher (17 s).

## 10 November 2026 (morning) — SOG-0164: the page Maintenance opened in 2.5 s

- **Measured** (the user's launcher, restarted at 8:56, by Simplification): two calls of `GET /__setup/changes` in a row, 593 ms without `since`, 703 ms with it, nothing changed. **Found**: `frame_tree` walked `data/` with `os.walk` and, for each of the 10 700 files, `islink`, `isjunction` and `stat` (three system calls). Measured here on the same folder: 350–380 ms for the walk.
- **Done**: the walk by `os.scandir` (on Windows the size, the date and the reparse-point attribute — link or junction, never followed — come with the listing): **42 ms warm** for the 10 671 files of the user's `data/` (the head included), 20–25 ms for the walk alone. The tree computed less than **1 s** ago (2 s for a device of the local network) is served again to any caller: two calls in a row walk `data/` once; a batch of the frame clears it (the write that changes `data/` stays visible at once). The launcher reads the file hashes **once at start** in the background (a cold read of 10 000 files: 750 ms), so the first page does not pay for it. Rejected: a tree hash kept until a write of the launcher or a sign of the repository (`.git/index`): the files that the user or another tool changes in the folder would be missed, and the walk is now cheap. K: I39 (red before: 380 ms for 6 000 files; green: well under 50 ms, same heads and trees, a junction not followed).
