## 10 November 2026 (afternoon) — SOG-0161: the route engine becomes ours (`tools/route_engine` is the reference; `E:/DEFA/rad` is archived)

**Plan, written before the code** (what the card asks, and what is done of it):
1. *Layers.* Cut the trip into SID / LOW / HIGH / FRA / STAR. **Done as a reading of the result**: `layered.py` classes every leg and groups them into sections; the search itself still runs on the one graph of the engine (airways + FRA links + connectors), whose edges already carry their layer.
2. *The terminal, without SID or STAR.* **Done**: when no SID / STAR is read for an aerodrome, the engine joins it to the FRA gates that the EUROCONTROL book **names for that aerodrome** (« D » departure point, « A » arrival point, the aerodrome in the row), beyond the 40 NM radius, **only if the climb can reach the floor of the area**: FL285 in Sweden needs (28 500 − elevation) / 2 000 ft/min × 450 kt ≈ 107 NM; a nearer gate that is on no airway is dropped, a farther one by more than 120 NM is not a connector; the six best placed (least detour towards the other aerodrome) are kept (all of LFPG's 20 made the search blow its budget). The connector stays a simulation DCT, said « SID non lue ».
3. *Intermediate points instead of a global fallback.* **Not changed**: `recovery.propose` (direct, pieces, regional FRA) stays the last resort; the new connectors made it unnecessary for the reference case. To do if a case needs it.
4. *A DCT only within the RAD's limit.* **Partly**: a DCT is `controlled` only if the RAD's annex 3B DCT publishes the pair, available, without condition, at all hours; any other DCT is `fallback`, « non contrôlé ». **The horizontal DCT limit by airspace (annex 3B FRA LIM, « 0NM », « FRA ») is not applied**: it needs the airspace outlines the books do not carry.
5. *Result by section and share controlled.* **Done**: `control` on each result (INTEGRATION « Route computation »).
6. *Climb time bounds the first FRA point.* **Done** (point 2): the engine already knew the rule for refusing; it now uses it to choose.
7. *Speed.* ESSB → LEMO: **27–42 s before, 7–11 s now** (the FRA graph cost 12 of 16 s: the distances are computed on pre-computed sines and cosines, the goals' distances once, the candidate lists once per 1° cell, and in the normal search only the points whose detour from the legs is under 250 NM are vertices; `wide`, the plan's second automatic try, keeps all). The target « < 10 s » is met on a quiet machine, not always (the run varies by ± 2 s with the other sessions' load).
- **Reference case**: ESSB → LEMO at FL350 now gives `ESSB DCT ABAMA DCT TOKSI … DCT DEMIR N872 MEDIL DCT PITHI … DCT KETEX DCT LATEK … DCT BLN DCT LEMO`, 1 645 NM (1 612 NM direct), status « CONTRÔLE PARTIEL — À VÉRIFIER »: Borealis FRA, then N872 DEMIR → MEDIL (a published airway), then the French and the Iberian FRA (KETEX … BLN as the user saw), connectors SID / STAR in simulation. It is **not** the user's ARVID T424 PPN N857 NOLSA N10 BAN N857 HIJ N10 SVL: the engine takes the shortest chain of FRA links and one airway; the Spanish upper network is in the graph and not preferred. 22 % of the distance is controlled (the airway), 65 % are FRA candidates, 14 % fallback.
- **Found, not mine to fix tonight**: LFPG → LEMD fails on the user's data at the **committed** engine too (« Budget de recherche atteint (6 s / 100 000 états) », 32 s), where RouteSim's note had it in 3.7 s via Y129 · T430: the data moved (Spain's 29 Oct base is empty of airways and the engine falls back to 1 Oct) or the budget is too tight for the graph; to look at with the user's workstation. K125 (red before, on a fixture: no airway near either aerodrome).

## 10 November 2026 (evening) — SOG-0161, the rest: why LFPG → LEMD failed, airways preferred, partial routes shown

- **(a) LFPG → LEMD, cause found.** Without FRA the same request takes **1.4 s** (89 % controlled, `… URUNA Y129 DITOP T430 GASMO N867 WUSHI`, the route of RouteSim's note); with FRA it hit the budget (32 s). **The default FRA preference was +10 %** (`planning.normalize`, RouteSim 4): a free-route link costs 90 % of its length, so (1) the engine preferred a direct to the published airways, and (2) the A* heuristic (geodesic distance × the cheapest cost per NM, then 0.9) loosened by 10 %: on a long continental route the non-dominated altitude labels exploded (100 000 states). **Fix**: the default is **−10 %** (`DEFAULT_FRA_PREFERENCE`), a free-route link costs 110 %; the heuristic is tight again. LFPG → LEMD: **1.7 s**, 89 % controlled. A positive preference is still accepted (the range is −30 % to +50 %) and the budget may then be reached on a long route. K133 (red before: default +10 %, the free route wins over the airway).
- **(c) Airways preferred when they exist.** The same change: a published airway wins over a free-route direct when its detour is under 10 % (the plateau holds from −5 % to −20 %). **ESSB → LEMO**, user's data, FL350: before **22 %** controlled / 65 % incomplete / 14 % fallback (a chain of FRA links); now **65 % controlled / 22 % incomplete / 13 % fallback**, 1 667 NM (1 645 before, direct 1 612), 6–8 s, flying `ESSB DCT ELPAX … DEMIR N872 MEDIL DCT DUCRA UM728 KOTAP J18 VEROS G54 CTX G28 LMG R10 BTZ R299 DONOS M299 PPN N10 CJN L27 NASOS N869 ANZAN L27 BLN DCT LEMO`: the French and Spanish upper network, **including PPN N10 like the user's variant** (not his exact ARVID T424 PPN N857 NOLSA N10 BAN N857 HIJ N10 SVL: his route is on other airways of the same network; the engine takes the shortest). LFPO → EIDW 78 % controlled (476 NM), LFPG → LFMN 38 %.
- **(b) Iteration by intermediate points instead of the global fallback: not rewritten, shown.** `recovery.propose` already cuts the trip into pieces joined at points near the great circle (airway pieces, DCT candidates, a regional FRA fallback); what was missing is that its result reached nobody but a sentence. The answer of the screen now carries the proposals as **alternatives marked `proposal: true`, `incomplete: true`, with their `control`**, on the **opt-in** `proposals: true` of the request (without it the contract of K106 holds: no route computed, no alternative). K134.
- **(d) The RAD's DCT limit by FIR: not done, and why.** « Annex 3B FRA LIM » gives a horizontal DCT limit per **ACC / airspace code** (`EDGGACC` 40 NM, `EBBUACC` 50 NM below FL115 and 0 NM FL115–245, `LFFRAC_FRA` FRA above FL195…) with level windows, exceptions by flight type and by aerodrome, in free text: applying it needs the outline of each ACC sector by level, which no source in the suite carries. The FIR contours of openAIP (parser 26) are a start for a coarse « which FIR is this DCT in », not for the sector limits; until then a DCT stays « non contrôlé » unless the RAD's annex 3B DCT publishes the pair.

## 10 November 2026 (noon) — SOG-0178: a slow outside service must never freeze the launcher

- **What blocks today, in numbers** (read in `tools/launch.py`, then measured on the real server class with a host that does not answer): **the server is not the bottleneck**: `Server` is a `ThreadingHTTPServer` (one thread per connection) and the relay of `POST /__setup/net` (`frame_net`) holds no lock; ten relays on a dead host held **ten threads and ten connections for 20 s each** (the old timeout), and the launcher's other requests were still answered in a few milliseconds. **What froze was the browser**: the launcher speaks HTTP/1.0 (one connection per request), Chrome opens **six connections per origin at most**, and six relays waiting 20 s on `api.open-meteo.com` take all of the origin's connections; the pages, the files and `/__setup/` of the same page wait behind them (BUGS SOG's suspicion). The jobs' calls (`[sortant] échec travail : overpass-api.de … HTTP 502`) run in the jobs' own thread and are not involved (a job's slow host holds the one job slot, not the pages).
- **Done**: the relay is **short** (connection 5 s, each read 15 s, the whole answer 15 s: a service that drips is cut), **bounded** (two at a time per host, **four in all**: two of the browser's six connections always stay for the pages) and **refuses the surplus at once**: `503`, `X-SOG-Reason: busy`, `Retry-After: 2`, body `{ error: 'occupé', busy: true }`, in a few milliseconds; the **reason is readable** in `X-SOG-Reason` on a failure (`timeout`, `reset`, `refused`, `unreachable`, `http-<code>`) next to the unchanged `502 unreachable` body and `X-SOG-Offline`. No access widened, no CSP changed. **K136** on the real server class: ten relays → two leave, eight are refused in under 400 ms; six relays on three hosts → four leave; the other requests answer in under 400 ms meanwhile; red before (ten left, none refused).
- **For V3 Frame** (the client of `/__setup/net`): a `503` with `busy` means « try again in a moment », not « offline »; do not retry in a tight loop, and do not queue more than two relays per host on the page's side.

## 11 November 2026 — SOG-0238: the personal-use tick is held by the launcher

**Decision** (the user said yes to « le lanceur lit lui-même la coche »; the ergonomics session validated the design the same day). The tick « Je certifie utiliser les données pour mon usage personnel » lived in the page cache (`cache/data/personal.json`) and every call declared `personal: true`: any page code could pass it. The launcher now holds it itself.

- **File**: `data/local/personal_use.json` `{ certified: bool, at: ISO date, migrated?: true }` — `data/local/` is served to nobody (`hidden()`), never synchronised, never committed; written by the launcher only (`tools/personal_use.py`).
- **Route** `/__setup/personal`, this workstation only (a device of the local network and the relay: 403): `POST` with a STRICT body `{ certified: true | false }` (anything else, another field or type: 400); `GET` gives `{ certified, at }`. One launcher console line at each change (« usage personnel certifié » / « usage personnel retiré »), nothing else.
- **Migration, once**: a tick already in the page cache is carried to the launcher when the Data screen opens and the launcher has no file: `POST { certified: true, migrated: true }` (the only second body allowed; 409 `exists` when the file is there) — the file carries `migrated: true` (the carrying of a gesture the user already made, dated). After that only a click writes it. With a launcher, the box of the screen shows the launcher's file (`GET`), not the cache.
- **`proc/lnm`** reads THIS file and ignores `personal` from the page (the argument is still accepted, without effect): `not-personal` without the real tick. Counter-proofs K147 (`personal: true` from the page without the file: refused; with the tick: read) and K173 (route, strict body, 403 for the local network, one console line, migration once).

**What it is worth, said as it is.** A script of the SAME origin can call the write route as well, so this is not a defence against a hostile page script; the gain is against forgetfulness and against page code that declares the tick in the place of the user. A stronger guarantee would need a gesture on a page served by the launcher itself: not proposed.

**What remains (not in this lot, the day before the user's acceptance test)**: `base/fetch` and the other jobs still receive `personal: [sources]` (ENAIRE, NATS, AirNav, LFV…) from the page. They should read the launcher's tick too; to carry to the report.

## 12 November 2026 — SOG-0245: the survey of the lock, then the contract

**Survey (what could be read).** The launcher keeps only the LAST job in `tools/.regen_last.json`: the log of the evening of 11 Nov is gone, so *which* operation held the lock cannot be proved. What the file says: the last starts that evening were `base/fetch` 18:04, `base/pickup` 18:06 and `base/build` 19:52, followed by `stats/output` 20:13 and `route/fra-points` 20:15 — each of those needs the slot, so the build had ended by about 20:09: no lock was left by it. The operations that hold the slot longest: `base/build` (a headless browser, minutes), `weather/world` (up to 900 s) and `ground/fetch`, which the page starts BY ITSELF after the procedures and which holds the slot for each terrain. **`tools/base_upload.py` refused a file whenever ANY job ran** (`job.state == 'running'`), the ground read included: the refusal of his Portugal file by « un travail du poste tourne » is plausible, but it is not the whole story — the Portugal row had no « Choisir le fichier… » at all (SOG-0244) and `base/pickup` knows only SIA, NATS and LFV, so « prendre le fichier téléchargé » could not work for it either. Two bugs, perhaps, not one.

**Decision.** Every « busy » refusal carries the operation that runs (name, start, step, progress, vitals); a job that gives no sign of life for 300 s is `stuck` and can be released by the launcher after it has checked it (thread gone or silent, processes it declared killed); cancel stays the cooperative stop. Contract in INTEGRATION (« The running operation »). Not done: a persistent log of the starts and ends of the jobs (so that the next time the question can be answered from a file).

## 12 November 2026 — SOG-0244: the eAIP of Portugal taken by hand (zip carrying an ISO, ISO, folder), and its first real trial

**Can an ISO be read in pure Python? Yes.** The user's two files (`EAIP ISO.zip`, `EAIP ISO (1).zip`) are zips of ONE member, an ISO 9660 level 1 image (`CD001`, one volume descriptor, no Joliet, no Rock Ridge, no UDF); the names are cut to 8.3, so the pages are found by their title. `tools/iso9660.py` (read-only, bounded) reads the member of the zip in place in about two seconds.
**First real trial of the Portugal reader (12 Nov, read only, nothing of his files in the tests): it holds.** Edition of 1 Oct 2026 (his `EAIP ISO.zip` and his extracted folder): 435 of 435 rows of ENR 4.4 read as points, 107 legs in ENR 3.1 and 188 in ENR 3.3 (about 70 routes), 449 points and 295 legs in all. Edition of **26 Nov 2026** (his `EAIP ISO (1).zip`, not yet in force): the points are read (452) but the package writes **empty route designators** (`<p class="Route-designator …"/>`) for 73 route blocks, so only 71 legs are read: the routes the page does not name are said (`skipped.routes_without_name`), never guessed; the point rows of that edition use another class (`~frameAPSHiddenRow`), so the reader now takes the name and the position by the cells, not by the class. A limit « FL195 / » (a slash after the upper limit) is read. Choice of the edition: the latest in force, else the nearest to come, said.
**Not done**: a folder chosen through the browser (a folder cannot be sent as one file; the launcher finds an extracted folder in its own folders); other States' ISO packages (they would be profiles).

**SOG-0244, precisions of the ergonomics session (12 Nov).** (1) The two zips are two editions: `EAIP ISO.zip` (184 MB, 18:04) = **valid from 01 OCT 2026**, `EAIP ISO (1).zip` (219 MB, 19:52) = **valid from 26 NOV 2026**; none carries 29 Oct (NAV Portugal does not publish on every AIRAC). When several recognised files are there the launcher takes the **latest edition in force**, not the latest downloaded (K176: two zips, the one of 2099 is not taken). (2) The edition read is on the result line (`edition`), `future` marks an edition not yet in force, **`older_than_cycle`** marks one older than the AIRAC cycle in force (taken, signalled: the screen says « ce fichier est celui du cycle précédent », never a silent tick). (3) **No temporary ISO**: the image is read in place from the zip member (seekable stream), nothing is extracted, nothing is written in `data/local/upload/` but the file the user chose; the extracted folder is read at ONE known relative path (`eAIP/html/eAIP`, `html/eAIP` or the folder itself), never searched in depth, never a path from the page (K176: pages two levels down are not found).

**SOG-0244, the join with the screen (12 Nov).** `navpt` stays a source the launcher downloads, and the catalogue adds what the screen needs to say, from the start, that the Portugal line also takes a file: `steps` (the page `https://www.nav.pt/ais`, what to download, ×4) read by `AeroCatalogue.stepsOf`, and `fetch.pick = { glob, name }` — a field, not a change of `how` (`how: 'launcher'` is still what the launcher does; the screen reads `pick` to offer « Choisir le fichier… » without waiting for a first failure). The list « Sources prises par l'envoi » of the screen (`modules/data/upload.js` `TAKEN`) must name `navpt` (the upload recognises it: `expected` carries it). K177 joins, on the launcher side, the recognition of the upload and the download of the zone; the page side stays with L84.

## 12 November 2026 — SOG-0258: publishing a zone (A + B), said as it is

Per zone the launcher already had the download (`download`), the build (`state`, `reasons`, `builtCycle`, `parserVersion`) and a LOCAL COMMIT per build; it had no state of publication and no verb: publishing is the synchronisation of `data/`, which pushes every unsent commit. **A**: `base/status` gives `builtAt` and `publish { state, commit, at }` by the ancestry of the last build commit in the known remote branch (`tools/zone_publish.py`, four git calls, no network). **B**: `base/publish { zone, dry }`: the plan lists the other built zones that leave with it and the NUMBER of the user's files (never their names), then the real run is the synchronisation. There is **no isolation of a zone**: a push of a commit sends its unsent ancestors; isolating one zone (a branch, or a cherry-pick on the remote tip) is heavy and risky, and is put to the user (Q-0025). A sync in progress is not stopped by the verb (the SYNC_LOCK of the launcher serialises the passes).

## 12 November 2026 — SOG-0259: the list of the cycle (`base/cycle`)

The user asked (« une liste globale de tous les refreshs à faire, avec une coche de la part de l'outil ») and the ergonomics session accepted the contract with four precisions, all held: (a) `late` is distinct from `missing` (UK Mil of 6 Oct and the FAA classes of 1 Oct are taken, older than the cycle); (b) the NEXT cycle is apart (`next.items`: what Downloads already holds for 26 Nov, what is still to take, « will be taken when it is in force »); (c) a source with no reader is a line with its reason (`unread`: AIP Canada, Naviair, the XML of openAIP); (d) Downloads is swept by NAMES of the first level only, and a name that can be of two States is said as it is (`EK_` = Denmark OR Faroe). Run read-only on his workstation (0.4 s) it gives, for the cycle 2611: SIA, ENAIRE, AirNav, LFV, NATS, DFS, openAIP and the FAA layers `done`; UK Mil, DIRCAM and the FAA classes of 1 Oct `late`; and, for 2612, the NATS AIP of 26 Nov and the EUROCONTROL RAD and FRA files already in Downloads. A source published by editions (NAV Portugal) is not `late` for the cycle alone.

## 12 November 2026 — SOG-0260: why the screen said « 69 zones attendent un fichier » (two launcher faults), and SOG-0264: the source of the procedures

**The screen against the launcher.** On his workstation `base/status` (read only) answered, for a page that declares none of the personal sources, **84 zones `stale`, 239 reasons `personal-undeclared`**: the record of a build keeps the personal sources DECLARED at that build (airnav, enaire, lfv, nats, navpt: a global list) and the status flagged each of them in EVERY zone, Afghanistan included, which none of them feeds; `personal-undeclared` is `buildable: false`, so the page counted the zone as « waiting for a file ». Fixed: the code is given only for a source that feeds the zone (`sources_of`); with the same declaration the answer is now 72 `up-to-date` and 12 `stale` (8 `parser-old`: Cyprus, Egypt, Israel, Lebanon, Libya, Palestine, Syria and the United Kingdom; 5 `personal-undeclared` for the zones that really use a personal source: es, gb, ie, pt, se; 2 `source-unread`). Second fault: NAV Portugal publishes by EDITIONS (« valid from 01 OCT 2026 » stays in force until the next one), so its file dated 1 Oct was a cycle older than 29 Oct and the zone was `source-old` (« cycle passé, téléchargez le cycle 2611 »): `aero_cycles.EDITION_SOURCES` makes an edition in force count as the cycle in force. What is NOT a reason: `notices[] { code: 'no-points', source: 'openaip' }` (« zone sans points ni voies : la source ne les porte pas ») is a notice, not a state: it must not colour the zone.
**SOG-0264.** `base/coverage` already counted the aerodromes with procedures per zone; it now says where they come from (`procSources`: `eaip` = the SIA eAIP, `cifp` = the FAA, `ead-basic` = the EAD of EUROCONTROL). On his workstation: France and Andorra `eaip`, the United States and Canada `cifp`, Ireland `ead-basic`; Belgium and Georgia hold records whose source kind is not written. Little Navmap (this workstation only) is not a file: the page knows it through `lnmAvailable()`.

`steps` (the page, the named files, what each gives, ×4) now exist for every source a person takes by hand or may have to: SIA, NATS, LFV, NAV Portugal, EUROCONTROL (already), and **UK Mil** (`ENR-5-2.pdf`, the link the launcher's console gives), the **FAA NASR** (`<JJ>_<Mon>_<AAAA>_FIX|AWY|NAV_CSV.zip`, the page of the subscription), **NAV CANADA** (the DAH the launcher reads, and the AIP Canada files — `enreng…`, `adeng…` — seen and *not read yet*), **Naviair** — one entry for Denmark, Greenland and the Faroe Islands (`zones: dk, fo, gl`): `EK_ENR_3_1/3_2/3_3/3_4/4_1/4_4_en.pdf` (Denmark; the same names serve the Faroe Islands, whose pages are « NIL » for routes and points, and the browser adds « (1) » to the second), `BG_ENR_3_1/3_2/4_1/4_4_en.pdf` (Greenland). **Links verified** = those the user or the launcher gave (`aim.naviair.dk/en/`, the UK Mil ENR URL, the NASR page of the code) and those already in the catalogue; NAV CANADA has only its home page (no deep link was checked). **A fact the inventory must carry**: the user's `28DaySubscription_Effective_2026-10-29.zip` (the FAA full subscription) is not one of the three CSV sets the launcher takes; its `CSV_Data/29_Oct_2026_CSV.zip` would have to be opened for them (not done).

## 12 November 2026 — SOG-0273: UK Mil fallback and FAA class airspaces (not coded)

- **UK Mil (ENR 5.2)**: the source stays, shelved `unavailable` (publisher down for about a year; last file taken 6 Oct). Lead for a fallback, NOT coded: the full civil UK AIP PDF (`EG-aip-en-29-10-2026.pdf`, 772 MB) carries its own ENR 5.2; what would have to be read there: the low-flying areas (LFA 1A…7(T)), the TRA / MOA tables with their limits and times, and the air-to-air refuelling areas with their IP → CP axes — none of which the NATS AIXM carries (0 of the 56 zones of the UK Mil layer are in it; it only has 15 `AARA AREA nn` polygons). It needs a PDF text reader (not in the standard library): page-side, like the DIRCAM ENR 5.7.
- **FAA class airspaces**: `class_airspace_shape_files.zip` (and `Additional_Data/Shape_Files/Class_Airspace.shp` in the subscription) is the same layer name as the ArcGIS `Class_Airspace` the launcher takes, but NOT the same file: 5,621 polygons against 6,041, and 25 fields against 30 (no `STATE`, `ICAO_ID`, `CITY`…). Not substituted; a streamed shapefile reader (390 MB) and a mapping of the fields would be needed first.

## 12 November 2026 — Security review (audit line 165): zips opened before any count, FRA archive without ceiling, ISO without bound

- **Zips we did not write** (proved by Sécurisation on `aero_fetch.eaip_manual`: 250,000 entries = 138 MB of Python memory; a trapped 1.5 GB zip: several GB): `tools/zip_guard.open_zip` reads the entry count and directory size of the end record (zip64 included) before `zipfile` builds the directory; every opening of a zip in Downloads or in a deposit goes through it. Case: **K195** (a zip of `MAX_MEMBERS + 1` entries is refused by `open_zip` and an eAIP scan of a folder holding it returns nothing, no exception).
- **FRA archive without ceiling** (500 names = 500 files of up to 32 MB in `data/local/upload/`): only the workbook of the cycle in force and of the next are taken, TWO at most, each bounded to `TAKE_MAX` decompressed bytes; more is refused with its reason. Case: **K195** (500 names refused; three members → the two of the cycles).
- **ISO**: at most 600 ENR pages and 32,000 files, and a TOTAL reading time of 90 s (`ISO_MAX_SECONDS`) because each backward read of a deflated member restarts the decompression (about 5 s per read on 1 GB); the bound that plays is SAID (`ValueError`, message), never a silence. Case: **K195** (time bound).
- Not done: copying the deflated ISO member to a temporary file; the writes of a verb after `job.released`; `_trim` keys and non-text values.

### 12 Nov 2026 (evening) — the three leftovers of the SOG-0248 survey

- **Glyph-name fonts** (`proc_pdf._glyphs`): LFBD RNP A VPT 05 has no character table, so pypdf returns `/g50/g46/g48`. The set is ASCII − 32 (`/g1` a space, `/g136` the degree sign); a fragment is decoded when the names cover ≥ 60 % of it, a name outside the set stays as printed. Nothing else is guessed.
- **RF with the centre alone on its line** and the radius over two lines (`Radius` / `2,20NM`) or in one (`Radius 2,20NM`, comma and glued NM) read as the three-line layout of LFST (K200). The end point of the leg is the first word of the line after the centre.
- **A `TF` with no course, no distance, no turn, no overfly and no angle** that comes after legs of a group is an initial fix printed with the wrong terminator (LFBD RNP A VPT, ETPAR): it opens its own group as an IF, said in the warnings. It never prolongs the previous transition.
- **Spelled letters after a terminator** are one point (`IF V A G N A` is VAGNA, `VA` is a terminator only at the start of a row); `IF G TQ` is GTQ; `5-` / `-5` after the true course is the distance and an empty turn.
- **A lost dash on an APPROACH page**: when several dispositions fit, those that put an altitude under 500 ft in the minimum or maximum column are dropped (the rule `_fit_geometry` already applied); one left, the row is read. Departures keep their earlier guardrail (K25, LFPG OLZOM 7B).
- Cases **K210**, **K211**, **K212** (the printed rows; red before: no RF read, `VA` + `GNA`, `GTQ` and two `IST23` rows lost). Result on the cycle of 29 Oct 2026: LFBD RNP A VPT 05 and RNP AR Y 05 are `check` with their two arcs developed (43 points each, no gap), LFST INA RNAV 23 has its five transitions. Left in doubt: LFST SID STR1Z 23 (unread, not part of the survey).
- **Lost dash, second rule** (`_fit`): when several dispositions fit, those that print only one of the magnetic and the true course are dropped (the page prints both or neither); LFST SID 23 « CF LUPEN - 212 214.0 … » is read. The LFPG departure guardrail (K25, a lost dash with only a speed-like number) is unchanged. Case **K214**.
- **UK Mil vs NATS** (SOG-0273 point 5, measured on the 29 Oct 2026 cycle): the 56 areas of the military ENR 5.2 are not in the NATS set (0 by designator, 0 by name, 5 by outline ≥ 0.7, all coincidences); the NATS set carries the AIAA, MTA, AARA and TRA of the civil AIP. « UK Mil » stays unavailable at the publisher; no reader of the civil PDF; the catalogue note says both halves (**K213**).
- **Bounds on numbers read from a PDF** (audit 170): `GLYPH` takes one to three digits, the RF radius one or two integer digits and at most 50 NM (`RADIUS_MAX`, `_radius`), the accuracy cell three digits; a value outside the bound is not read, never inf. Case **K215** (red before: ValueError).
- **Non-finite numbers** (SOG-0283): RF radius range **]0 ; 50] NM** (an arc of the French charts is a few NM; 50 leaves room for a wide arc and shuts out a shifted cell), distance 1–3 integer digits and 3 decimals, vertical angle 2 integer digits, accuracy 3 digits; a value outside is not read (the cell stays empty; for the radius the leg is kept in doubt `rf-arc`). Backstop: `tools/regen/proc.py` writes `json.dumps(..., allow_nan=False)`, on ValueError the aerodrome is said in the log and its file is not written (the page would otherwise get an invalid JSON). Counter-proof **K216**: before the bounds a 400-digit distance gave `inf` and a 400-digit vertical angle `-inf` in the legs (measured); after, every leg is finite and the whole reading serialises with `allow_nan=False`. Checked: `proc_arc.py` and `proc_geometry.py` convert nothing from text (they take the legs), `proc_cifp` / `proc_ead` / `proc_manual` / `proc_ocr` are other readers, not touched by this lot.

## 12 November 2026 — SOG-0102: Portugal and Ireland in the GeoNames extract

- **What was missing**: `shared/carto/villes_geonames.csv` (read by `SiaBase.cities()`: the place search, the turning-point names, the map labels, and the publication by zone) held 2 towns for Portugal (Miranda do Douro, Sendim) and 43 for Ireland (15,000 inhabitants and more only), while France, Spain or Germany carry every inhabited place of 500 inhabitants and more (the cities500 criterion).
- **Source and licence**: the GeoNames country extracts `PT.zip` and `IE.zip` (`download.geonames.org/export/dump/`, the host of the `geonames` entry of the catalogue, CC BY 4.0, credited in `LICENSE.md` and in the catalogue). No new host.
- **What it gives**: class P (inhabited places), 500 inhabitants and more: Portugal 1,025 (of 16,615 class P rows: GeoNames carries no population for most freguesias, 15,210 are at 0), Ireland 567 (of 12,159, 11,163 at 0). Rows 70,962 → 72,509; 4,115,964 → 4,132,375 bytes. The same tool as the overseas territories did it: `python tools/update_cities_geonames.py PT IE` (replaces the rows of the two countries, adds none of the world).
- **Seen after**: `tools/check_cities.py` lists 63 more towns outside their contour: the Azores and Madeira (the simplified Portuguese contour has no islands, the same class as Jeju or Hawaii, 22 October), Sagres (south-west tip), Moville (Inishowen, on the border). They are filed by their country (`cc`) as decided on 22 October; the contour is not a defect of the extract.
- **Case K217**: Portugal ≥ 900 and Ireland ≥ 500 towns; every row has a finite latitude and longitude in range, an integer population, a two-letter country and a readable name; no geonameid twice. Red before (PT 2, IE 43).
- **Not done**: the same measure for the other countries of the world is not made (only the zones' neighbours were looked at); the refill is a developer action (the extract is in the repository), not a button of the Data screen.
- **13 Nov 2026 — the Little Navmap card only exports** (SOG-0023, § 10.35; written by Features SOG): after 5 Nov nothing ran by
  itself, but the card's « Synchroniser maintenant » still made ONE two-way pass — a file changed in Little Navmap updated the sheet, the
  newest value won, a file of Little Navmap alone became a sheet — without the preview the rule asks for. The card now offers « Exporter
  les fiches » (`exportNow()` = `sync('export', { exportOnly: true })`): sheets without a file get one, a changed sheet rewrites its own,
  a removed sheet's unchanged file is set aside; a changed file, a file of Little Navmap alone, and a sheet whose file name is taken by
  an unlinked file are listed (`state.changed`, shown on the card with « à importer par Appareils › Importer un fichier »), never merged
  nor overwritten. `sync()` and its five cases stay for the bench (test/lnmperf.html) and the opt-in trial. K218 (red before: two sheets
  written by the button).

## 13 November 2026 — SOG-0298: landing aids of an aerodrome (ILS and LOC first)

- **Asked** (user, trial of SOG-0203): an approach known by its chart only must be choosable, with the frequency of its aid and a « Carte » button limited to that procedure in « Aides radio ». The ergonomics session decided the first step: ILS and LOC only, official source only, nothing from X-Plane.
- **What the base already held**: the SIA AIXM 4.5 gives 100 ILS on 73 aerodromes, each with aerodrome, runway, identifier, localizer frequency, axis and whether a DME is associated (`navaids.json`, `kind: 'ILS'`). The test aerodromes: LFOE EVX 22 (111.3), LFBD BD 23 and BEI 29, LFST ENT 05 and STZ 23, LFRS NT 03. VOR (98), NDB (97), DME (110) and TACAN (19) carry a frequency but no aerodrome: nothing is said about which one a VOR approach uses (no « nearby beacons »); reading AD 2.19 waits for the user's word.
- **Done**: `navaidsOf(icao)` on the contract (SPEC table): the entries of the aerodrome only, each field checked (the base is untrusted data), duplicates once, sorted. K227.
- **Kept at compilation, which the base used to drop**: `cat` (codeCat: `I` 61, `III` 29, `II` 5, `NOCAT` 5 on the cycle of 29 Oct 2026), `gs` (Igp, 97 of the 100), `dmeId` and `dmeChannel` (86 have a DME). Present only when the AIXM gives them: **the shape of an existing record does not change**, a reader that ignores them is unaffected. K226.
- **Not changed**: `SiaBase.VERSION` and `PARSER_VERSION` stay at 28 (an additive field is not a new format; bumping would mark every built zone « outdated »). The new fields appear at the next build of the zone (the cycle of 26 Nov, or a rebuild of `fr` on the workstation); `navaidsOf` answers without them meanwhile. The ergonomics session may decide to bump.
- **Count asked for the screen survey** (real files `proc/manual`, cycle 29 Oct 2026; `approachSummary` / `chartOnly`): LFOE 3 coded + 1 chart only (VPT RWY 04); LFBD 5 + 17; LFST 4 + 5; LFRS 3 + 8. Among the chart-only ones, those with an ILS or LOC in the name: LFBD 4 (ILS LOC 23 and 29, INA ILS VOR 23 and 29), LFST 3 (ILS LOC 05, ILS CAT123 LOC 23, INA ILS LOC VOR 23), LFRS 3 (ILS CAT123 LOC Y and Z 03, INA CONV ILS LOC 03), LFOE 0; VOR charts: LFBD 5, LFST 2, LFRS 2. `chartOnly` also lists the aerodrome's « 05-23 ENV » and « 11-29 ENV » charts at LFBD (names that are not approaches: seen, not filtered here).

## 13 November 2026 — SOG-0297: names of the holding points of the ground layer

- **Asked** (user, trial of SOG-0203: « K et w22 illisibles, ce sont des points d'arrêt, cartouche rouge »): the ground layer already tells a holding point (`kind: 'hold'`, a point, from `aeroway=holding_position`) from a taxiway (`twy`, a line); the drawing is BUGS SOG's. This lot is the data side only.
- **Rule, at the source** (`tools/regen/ground.py`, `_name`): for a holding point, « NO ENTRY » (any case) is not a name; a label « Point d'arrêt », « Point d'attente », « Holding point » or « Holding position » in front of a sign is taken off (« Point d'arrêt A » → « A »), before the 16-sign cut; a label alone gives no name. The `ref` still comes first. Taxiways, stands and aprons are untouched. K228.
- **Touched on the files already read** (7 aerodromes, 125 holding points): 8 « NO ENTRY » at LFPO and 1 « Point d'arrêt A » at LFBD, so 9. Not touched: LFOE (16), LFST (11) and LFRS (11) have no name for any holding point (OpenStreetMap has none there): they stay unnamed, drawn by their kind.
- **Signs with a runway** (« W28@02/20 », « W31@07/25 »…): 14 holding points of LFPO among the 125 read (counted wrongly as 11 in the first version of this note). The sign is the part before the « @ »: decided by the ergonomics session, see below.
- **Takes effect** when an aerodrome's ground is read again (job `ground/fetch`, « Réessayer » on the map): the files already in `proc/ground/` keep their names until then. The launcher loads the verb when it starts: a launcher already running keeps the old rule until it is restarted.
- **Same day, after the ergonomics session's answer**: the sign « W28@02/20 » becomes name « W28 » and a field `rwy: '02/20'` (`_name_rwy` in `tools/regen/ground.py`: one or two runways, two digits and L, R or C, spaces removed; an « @ » not followed by a runway leaves the name as it is; taxiways untouched); `ground()` hands the field on for a holding point only, after the same check (K229). K228 red before. Takes effect when the aerodrome's ground is read again.
- **chartOnly and the environment charts** (SOG-0298): the SIA files « RWY_ 05-23_ENV » and « RWY_ 11-29_ENV » (LFBD) among the IAC; they are not approaches and are no longer listed (K163: an ENV chart in the fixture, red before). Looked at, the words of the IAC chart names of 10 aerodromes (LFBD, LFBO, LFOE, LFPG, LFPO, LFPT, LFRN, LFRS, LFST, LFLL and EBBE, EIDW): FNA, INA, RNAV, RNP, ILS, LOC, VOR, CAT123, VPT, GNSS, AR, CONV, Y / Z / X / A / B, named fixes (MOPAR, BANOX…), NIGHT, EAST / WEST — all approaches — and ENV, the only one that is not. File names are irregular (« RWY_ 05-23_ENV » and « CAT_ 1_2_3 » carry a space): the pattern of `mine` already accepts it.
